The question on the draft law on farmers' cooperatives is addressed to the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development. The question is asked by Mr Jan Krzysztof Ardanowski. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u8	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Cooperatives in Europe and the world have played a very important role in organising economic life in Europe since the founding of the Association of Fair Pioneers. She played a particular role in the countryside, also in Polish territory in all three partitions was a basic form of economic activity. Stefczyk in Austria, Fr. Lorenzo in Prussian or Fr. Blizinski in the Russian occupation, to name only the most important ones, were pioneers, taught farmers about their own welfare. Besides, the Polish name, the equivalent of the cooperative, is a cooperative. When a proper name was sought, it was determined that this was a joint action. This is also what Stanisław Staszic described as cooperativeism - as a joint action, joint management of poverty. After the war in communist times, the idea of cooperativeism was distorted. In fact, what was called cooperatives in Poland was derived from the Soviet Union and had little to do with cooperative rules. There is no doubt that cooperatives also play an important role in Europe today - for example the General Committee for Agricultural Cooperatives, which is one of the main centres of farmers' influence on the European Union. It seems to me that regardless of the pathology that has grown around cooperatives over the years, this form should be supported in Poland, it should be developed. This legal formula did not work out and I think it could continue to serve farmers well. For this, legislative action is needed to modernise the application of the cooperative principle in agriculture. I would like to know what the Ministry of Agriculture is doing. [[♪ The bell ♪]] on the draft law on farmers' cooperatives.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u9	Thank you very much, sir. The question is answered by the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, Minister Jacek Bogucki. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u10	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your question, I would like to inform you that the Ministry of Agriculture has already developed a working version of the draft law on farmers' cooperatives, which will shortly be the subject of internal and then external consultations and further work. The expected effect of the measures proposed in the Farmers' Cooperatives Act will be to improve the efficiency of the management of agricultural producers. As the Member has said, in other countries of the European Union, such institutions have been working successfully for years, and in Poland, with such a large fragmentation of agricultural holdings, it is particularly important to introduce solutions that will deepen the consolidation process at the level of agricultural producers, so that our agriculture is more competitive so that farmers can compete successfully on national and European markets. The aim of the solutions will be to create incentives for farmers to appoint entities which would operate on the basis of the principles of cooperative law, and these incentives would in particular be to exclude those who would become farmers' cooperatives from certain fiscal obligations. In addition, incentives of such a nature were also envisaged for cooperatives so that farmers' cooperatives would be further consolidated. The main purpose of such cooperatives would be to conduct business for their members' holdings by planning agricultural production, adapting the scale and quality of this production to market expectations, concentrating the supply of their members' products, and concentrating the demand for the means of production needed for cooperative members' holdings. In addition to these activities, farmers' cooperatives for their members could process agricultural products, supply their members with products, store them, store them and standardise them, trade their members and products from their processing, provide services to their members related to their production of agricultural products. In addition, they could disseminate environmentally beneficial cultivation methods, production technologies, waste management methods among their members. One of the basic requirements that such a cooperative would have to meet is its participation as members of a minimum of five producer farmers in the sector in which it will specialise. It would be possible for a farmer to belong only to one cooperative in a given segment, the production sector. In addition, cooperatives would be obliged to create funds, including a mutual fund, which would be collected and used by associations of farmers' cooperatives for the development and promotion of these entities. The indivisible reserve fund to which each cooperative will be obliged to set up will be transferred to the general cooperative in the event of its liquidation. Such a solution in the form of a cooperative law should actually apply in Poland for years. Unfortunately, we lost many years because in Poland farmers were able to come together on a general basis in cooperatives, but such cooperatives were subject to general tax and business obligations such as other legal entities. This did not encourage the creation of farmers' cooperatives, it put Polish farmers in a more difficult competitive situation on the domestic market and on the European market. We hope that this law will be passed in a short time so that farmers can benefit from its benefits and create cooperatives for farmers. Farmers who are already more and more interested in association are increasingly seeing the need for such association in relation to the dominant position of processing and trade in the agri-food chain, will certainly benefit from this law and be interested in its entry into force. I hope that in the course of further work on this law, we will refine the project so that it meets the expectations of the whole environment. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u11	Thank you very much, Minister. Please, an additional question - Mr Ardanowski. You're welcome.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u28	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! I will try to answer these very detailed questions, and I will also give you detailed written information so that there is no mistake about the numbers, because not all the numbers I keep remembering, although I can quote a significant part of them in a moment. As far as the situation in the milk sector is concerned, the Ministry of Agriculture has been taking action on the situation on the milk market since the start of the Beata Szydło government and Krzysztof Jurgiel became Minister. Already at the turn of November and December we asked for an analysis of this situation within the European Union. We have asked for this again and there has been such a re-debate in February. The next debate will be next week, May 17th. These are debates taking place at the initiative of Poland, because the milk market is covered by the common organisation of the markets of the European Union. Therefore, a significant part of the support mechanisms must be implemented either at Union level or with the consent of the European Commission. This difficult situation on the milk market is mainly due to the situation on world markets: China's lower demand, the Russian embargo. This combined resulted in a fall in prices on global and European markets, although unfortunately in Poland we are seeing prices lower than they are on average in the European Union - at the moment about 8%. The national cattle population last year amounted to 2134 thousand. Pieces. This head fell, but milk production increased, so the productivity of Polish cows increased. When it comes to buying milk in 2015, we concentrated 10,872,000. t milk. It's an upward trend, because it was a 2.6% higher buy-in. than in the previous year, 2014 This growth trend continues in the first quarter of this year. Unfortunately, milk prices are falling, as I said, because they are currently lower than the European average by 8,5%. , as well as a 13% lower year-to-year rate - so this year, compared to the previous year, they are 13% lower. Unfortunately, this trend is all over Europe. Among the actions proposed by Poland... We have proposed to allocate additional financial resources to support milk producers, to increase intervention prices for butter and milk powder, to step up efforts to acquire new markets outside the European Union and to apply subsidies, export subsidies to third markets, to take intensive action to abolish the Russian embargo at European Union level, and, as you mentioned, we have taken action on the possibility of redemption, the abolition of penalties, the fees for exceeding milk quotas in the last quota year, 2014/2015. At present, this possibility of amending legislation at European Union level has been rejected. We request the possibility of introducing a special programme for Poland, allowing individual redemption of these fees for individual farmers in difficulty. Depending on whether the European Commission agrees to such a mechanism, we would like to apply this mechanism and seek to agree to it. The situation in which prices for milk in Poland are lower than on average in the European Union is also due to the lack of regulation on the supply chain of food products, which in Poland has not been regulated by the previous government. This case was not raised or tried to be regulated by law. This has been tried in terms of voluntary, good commercial practices, but without success. That is why we are also taking action on regulation and statutory and voluntary relations between the farmer, the agri-food processor and trade, because in the assessment not only of the Polish government, but also of the European Commission, action in this respect is insufficient. Unfortunately, we consider the European Commission's proposals insufficient, because the European Commission does not want to take much of these measures - especially those which would require EU funds to help farmers, as Poland is asking. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The actions proposed by the Commission shall be limited to actions financed by national budgets or to actions which do not require financial input. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u43	Thank you. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Minister Marek Tombarkiewicz. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u44	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In the Ministry of Health a draft law on amending the Act on Medical Activity and some other laws was prepared. It was referred to the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers for consideration. One of the proposed changes is the introduction of restrictions on the disposal of private parties' shares or shares of medicinal entities in the form of capital companies created and operated by a local government unit or State Treasury. A public entity and, respectively, a local government unit or State Treasury could dispose of shares or shares of their companies, provided that the nominal value of their shares or shares is not less than 51% the share capital of the company. This solution will prevent the transfer of shares or shares of capital companies of the Treasury or units of local government carrying out medical activities. Having a majority stake or shares will give public entities a decisive influence on the functioning of medicinal products, which is necessary for the performance of the tasks of health security, quality and availability of health services under Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. A company carrying out a medical activity shall not be created to benefit from the ownership of its shares or shares by selling them, but to provide the necessary health benefits. The measures taken prior to the entry into force of the proposed law aimed at the disposal of shares or shares of companies of the State Treasury and local government units will be subject to the existing provisions and therefore the proposed law is without prejudice to the so-called law. the interest in progress and therefore does not confer on the right of ownership under Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The other changes proposed by the Act are, in turn, a change in the procedure in the event of a net loss, i.e. a negative financial result of an independent public health institution, and a waiver of the option to convert SPOZ into a capital company in such a situation; the introduction of the principle of non-payment of dividends in capital companies in which the State Treasury, local government unit or medical university hold more than 50% shares or shares; strengthening the supervisory tools of the entity constituting its independent public health care facility; introducing the possibility of financing health benefits by local authorities units; resignation from the obligation to have medical accident insurance; allowing the possibility to transfer obligations and powers of the entity constituting the SPOZ; allowing the possibility to create new SPOZs; clarifying the rules on the supervision of medicinal entities; amending the provision on the management of SPOZ assets; extending the period to cover net loss by the entity which is currently established by the Act, up to 9 months from the expiry of the deadline for the approval of the financial statement; regulating the rules of cooperation of medicinal entities with the Police in the context of the search for missing persons; replacing the obligation to submit to the authority carrying out the records of health professionals issued by the State Sanitary Inspection Authority and insurance policies with the applicant's declaration of compliance with the requirements for premises and equipment and the conclusion of a contract of civil liability insurance; exempting the managers of medicinal entities from the obligation to provide the State Labour Inspection and the competent authorities of health professions with information on the basis of a medical profession or as a professional practice with which a medical agency has concluded the obligation to provide that the natural person with the natural agency to carry out of business. replacement of the term "medical establishment' and the term "negative financial result' with the term "net loss'. The project was preceded by a draft plan for the draft law amending the law on medicinal activities, which was the subject of public consultation and consultation, after which work on it was discontinued in connection with obtaining the approval of the Government's work programming team to prepare and process the draft law. The opinion and public consultation of the project ended on 4 May and on 11 May the project was adopted by the College of the Minister of Health and submitted to the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers for consideration. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u52	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! According to Art. Article 115 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 2 December 2009 on Medical Chambers of Chambers of Medicine, including the General Medical Chamber and the District Medical Chambers, shall receive from the budget of the State from the part of which the Minister responsible for Health is responsible financial resources to cover the costs of administrative activities relating to the performance of the following tasks. These are: the granting of a right to pursue a profession; the keeping of records of doctors, the register of medical practitioners in the field of medical practices under the rules laid down in the legislation on medical activities, the registers of post-graduate practitioners and the registers of doctors temporarily and occasionally engaged in the profession; the conduct of proceedings concerning the professional responsibility of doctors; the conduct of proceedings concerning incapacity to pursue the profession of doctor or insufficient preparation for the pursuit of the profession. Financial resources referred to in the above mentioned above. the laws on medical chambers shall be transmitted on the basis of contracts concluded between the Minister for Health and the District Medical Chambers and the General Medical Chamber. In the agreements of those chambers, they undertake to carry out specific tasks, while the Minister of Health undertakes to transfer funds of a certain amount to carry out those tasks. The two objectives were taken into account when setting the amount of the grants to the medical self-government provided for in the 2016 budget. The first is to implement the amount of the grant for the current year in such a way as to comply with the statutory obligation to refund to the local government the costs of carrying out tasks taken over from the state administration. The implementation of this objective is aimed at increasing the amount of grants for all, chief and 23 district doctors, to the amount of 6462 thousand. zł. The second objective is to close legal disputes with individual medical chambers for late payment of the costs of tasks carried out by local authorities in the years 2005 to 2015. It should be stressed that the grant for medical authorities, starting in 2004, remained virtually unchanged despite the increasing number of cases they dealt with. Since 2004, funds for grants to the medical self-government have increased from PLN 2 399 400 to PLN 2463 thousand. PLN in 2015, that is about 2,26% At the same time, the number of registered doctors and dentists increased significantly from 150 596 to 177 094 persons, i.e. it grew by 17.6% in these years Also the number of cases dealt with by medical authorities, e.g. by medical courts, increased more than twice - from 298 to 778 per year, i.e. 161% compared to 2004. As a result of the situation, the unit rates for the financing of the tasks imposed by the Law on a medical corporation, despite the increasing costs incurred by the medical institutions, were actually reduced annually. The difference between the amount of grants to the medical self-government provided for in the budget and the amounts resulting from the reports of the medical chambers exceeds about PLN 8 million per year. In the 2014 budget, the amount for grants amounted to 2463 thousand and the amount shown by the medical institutions in the annual reports amounted to more than 12 million PLN. As a result, the medical authority has been required, from 2007, to pay the funds equivalent to the actual costs incurred for carrying out the tasks, plus interest due and judicial costs. Between 2011 and 2015, the Ministry of Health received requests for a settlement test for a total amount of 23,522 thousand. PLN and has been brought before the courts of the medical chambers for various years and different chambers for a total amount of 5950 000. zł. Analysis of final judgments in cases in which chambers claimed damages under the provisions of the Civil Code, i.e. Article 2 417 § 1 of the Civil Code indicated that the courts, in principle, considered all expenditure actually incurred by the local government and having regard to the implementation of statutory tasks as justified. In the courts there was a uniform view that the amount of the grant to the medical self-government should be calculated in such a way as to cover the costs actually incurred by the local government and shown in the reports submitted to the health department. At the same time, it should be noted that the calculations of the Supreme Medical Chamber show that the total debt of the Minister of Health to the Supreme Medical Chamber for the years 2007 to 2015 amounted to PLN 8 014 229 and, in the face of 23 district medical chambers - to PLN 50 162 677. As a result of long and difficult negotiations carried out at the Ministry of Health, the Chief Medical Chamber and 23 District Medical Chambers agreed to waive court proceedings in exchange for a single transfer of PLN 10 million, of which PLN 1 229 748 would fall to the Chief Medical Chamber and the remaining amount, i.e. PLN 8 770 252 - to the District Chambers. Thus, the Chambers waived any claim they could bring against the State Treasury's health minister represented for the tasks performed in 2005 and 2015. As far as the functioning of other local authorities is concerned, and we are only talking about the nursing authorities at present, the amount of the grant is discussed and negotiated annually with the Chief Chamber of Nurses and Obstetrics. Part of this amount [[♪ The bell ♪]] is increased annually by the so-called. over-executions in the trials or proceedings which are being conducted by the chief chambers of nurses and midwives in cases directed against members of the nursing authority. This year this amount is at the same level as last year. On the other hand, none of the nurses' and midwives' chambers applied for judicial proceedings against the Ministry of Health for an increase in that amount or payment for judicial judgments, as such judgments are simply not yet available. Thank you very much.

It was then extended to 2007 and 2010. In the framework of this restructuring, aid of PLN 6.5 million was granted to the company, but this scheme was not effective and the company did not regain its permanent ability to compete on the market. The situation is still very difficult because the uncovered loss from previous years amounts to PLN 13 million, the equity capital is negative, and currently the company has liabilities vis-à-vis the state budget, which amounts to around PLN 3.5 million. In order to cover these liabilities, even though the liabilities to the city of Krasnystaw, the company had to transfer its property so as to pay only outstanding taxes on the property. We are dealing with an individual situation, but this is also an example of a wider phenomenon, when such companies were left to themselves in fact, without looking at what is happening to them, because the fact that they have public-law arrears causes the company not to participate in direct tenders. Therefore, all these global companies that use the services of CORA-TEX commission this company as subcontractors. Thus, the added value of such contracts, resulting from the acquisition of experience, from the promotion of the company brand, falls into completely different hands. I'm wearing uniforms. Profitability, which today is 2.6% for WPH , and in the case of CORA-TEX is negative, it should be close to 5% over the period of two to three years. First of all, what is important for us is that we will be able to exploit the production capacity, which is 82,000. outer clothing, or uniforms, a year. And it's already happening. The companies are already working together. The consolidation process is spread over time, but it has been undertaken Joint actions, joint contracts are implemented. I can therefore say that these 200 jobs will certainly be saved and the crew can be calm. And we hope that it will indeed be one of the most important players in this industry in Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u94	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Families are the most important social group on which the whole society is founded. The comprehensive support of Polish families and their security and stability has become one of the main tasks placed before the Government of Law and Justice. It was understood that Polish families expect concrete and rapid action on this issue. Therefore, already during the programme congress in February 2014, Law and Justice announced a family supplement in the form of 500 PLN for the second and the next child. The draft law implementing these benefits was presented during the election campaign in September 2015. The 500+ family law was adopted by the Sejm less than 3 months after the government was sworn in. This allowed the programme to enter into force on 1 April 2016. The most important task of the programme is to increase the sense of economic security of Polish families by supporting them in raising children. The money paid out will help parents to partially cover the costs of meeting their needs and raising a child. The aim of the programme is also to contribute to increasing fertility. Currently, the fertility rate in Poland is about 1.3 births per woman and, for comparison, in 1983 it was 2.42. This puts Poland currently in 216th place in 224 countries and dependent territories. Only big families are a chance to stop the demographic crisis. Undoubtedly, the 500+ family programme is also an opportunity to develop the country, because returning money can certainly boost the economy. The scheme provides for the payment of a benefit of PLN 500 for each second and subsequent own child, the spouse, appointed and under legal care until they are 18 years old. The witness can be received by parents, one parent, legal guardians and actual carers. The amount of the benefit was set at PLN 500 per child per month, with payment for: each second and subsequent child regardless of the income of the family; the first child, if the income per person does not exceed PLN 800; and for the first child, if the income per person does not exceed PLN 1200 and any child in the family is disabled. In total, approximately 3700 000 could be supported. Polish children. The possibility to submit applications in the municipality, via mail and via the Internet was created on the first day of operation of the programme, 1 April 2016. Applications have already been submitted by more than 2100 thousand. out of 2,700 thousand. entitled to provide parents and carers. Over 487,000. applications are submitted online. Most families have submitted applications as early as the first month of the programme's operation. More than 1650 thousand were deposited in the municipalities. applications, mostly in Mazowsze, Wielkopolska and Silesia. During the first month of operation of the government programme, the family of 500+. the decision and more than PLN 430 million were paid. Within a few weeks of the introduction of the benefit, I ask the Minister to provide detailed information on the programme. Surely we can say more about the implementation of payments by local governments, as well as about the efficiency and undoubtedly positive consequences of introducing this instrument of support for Polish families. We proceed with the examination of item 6 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the draft law amending the Law on Hunting Law (prints No 219 and 324). ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u198	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the draft amendment to the Hunting Law Act. On 15 January 2016, the Parliamentary draft law amending the Hunting Law Act, print No 219, entered into force. The 12th meeting of the Sejm on 25 February 2016 addressed the joint meeting of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. The Act amending the Law on Hunting Law aims primarily at rationalising the hunting economy and introducing clear rules of responsibility for agricultural damage caused by game hunting in agricultural crops by wild boars, elks, dwarfs, deer and deer. For many years, agricultural environments have been calling for a lack of objective estimation of losses and due compensation for damage caused to agricultural crops. A farmer on the line - a hunting wheel was very often in conflict. Last year, we saw them getting worse: agricultural protests, I will remind you, a green town under KPRM and numerous demonstrations in the field. In response to these problems, following numerous consultations with agricultural organisations and hunting circles, a group of Members presented a bill to resolve damages, including the State Treasury, which owns a free-living game and is responsible for the proper hunting economy. The Act aims to eliminate negative phenomena at the interface between farmers - hunting circles by increasing the involvement of the State Treasury in the process of estimating and eliminating damage caused by game, as well as partially paying compensation. A novelty here is the creation of a State Compensation Fund, which consists of contributions from hunting circles, subsidies from the state budget and other revenue generated for this fund, donations. The damage is estimated and paid according to the area of damage caused by the voivod. This solution is intended to ensure respect for the interests of farmers through arbitrary integration of voivodships into the estimate of damage, as well as financially support hunting circles in terms of their payments, through the State Compensation Fund. Hunting circles from their contributions are not able to cover properly the losses caused by the game due to the very large diversity of the resources of hunting circuits and types of live game. In view of the great merits of the Polish Hunting Union in the field of active conservation of nature, this law is intended to help in the proper arrangement of relations between hunters and farmers, with direct involvement of the State Treasury. The competent territorially responsible agricultural chambers have been included in the compensation process due to the place of injury. It is the agricultural chambers that will keep lists of experts and participate in the assessment of damage at the request of the farmer. On 9 March, at the joint meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on the Environment, where a draft law was submitted by the High House, amendments were tabled in the presence of numerous representatives of agricultural organisations. The most important and positive opinions of the joint committees concerned the Article. 1 of the amended law and are related to Article. 46, Art. 49 and Article 49 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 50 Law Hunting, where after art. 46 The following Article is inserted: 46a-46d, and from Article. 2 amended act. The amendments concerning the clarification of the way in which the damage is estimated, the creation of the compensation fund and the final provisions have been positively assessed by the committees together with the project as a whole. On the other hand, amendments concerning: damage caused by hares, changes in time limits for inspection and assessment of damage, differentiation of contribution due to the nature of the circuit - field, forest - increase of contribution in case of over-normative increase in the number of game in the circuit and deletion in Article. Point 6 and Article 1 50b. 7 were not recognised by the committee and were presented as minority requests at the request of the applicants. The report is available in print No 324. Applicants have been and are open to any proposals for changes aimed at the best possible regulation of this important issue - taking into account the interests of farmers and hunters, but also the interests of our national treasure, i.e. wild game. With a view to properly aligning relations in the areas of the oldest economic activity of man, i.e. hunting and agriculture, as well as taking into account the welfare of wild game, on behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on the Protection of the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, please adopt a law by the High House amending the Law on Hunting Law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u199	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Anna Paluch, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u200	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary draft law amending the Law on Hunting Law has been worked over by the Joint Committees: Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry, Agriculture and Rural Development. The joint committee's report is contained in print No 324. My club, the Law and Justice Club, is currently submitting a package of 9 amendments to the bill, which aims to clarify and clarify the provisions of the amended law. Amendment 1 refers to Amendment 1, i.e. Article. 46 Hunting Law Act. It states that the State Treasury is responsible for the damage caused by wild boars, elks, deer, dwarfs and deer, including in the case of protection for the whole year, while the owner or the manager of the hunting circuit is responsible for the damage caused in the execution of the hunt. The principle is obvious, for such a record will undoubtedly prompt hunters to behave responsibly on the hunt, because they will be the ones who will bear the consequences and be responsible for the damage involved. We propose that requests for inspection and damage assessment should be submitted to the competent voivode, but in view of the convenience that the applicants have claimed, the application may also be submitted to the voivode who is required to pass it to the voivode immediately, but not later than 3 days. In Amendment 2, the following Article is added: 46a-46d. And we also make an amendment to this change, which clarifies the provisions. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 46a there are provisions on inspection and assessment of damage: time limits, parties to proceedings, possibility of participation by the representative of the agricultural chamber. We in our amendment propose to shorten the time limit for the inspection from 14 to 7 days from the date of receipt of the application, as well as to introduce a time limit of 5 days for the notification of the participants of the inspection. In turn, in the article. 46b the content of the protocol drawn up after the examination, etc., has also been determined by the right of the party not satisfied with the findings of the protocol as regards the amount of the damage to be presented to the assessor. I think the amendments will be discussed in detail at the committee meeting. Art. 46c, on the other hand, concerns the preparation of a protocol for the examination and assessment of the damage. We propose there a change in the sound of the mouth. 2 and 3 to set a deadline of 7 days for the voivodship to consider objections to the protocol, including the opinion of the assessor. Art. 46d sets out the procedure for determining the amount of compensation, the conditions for reimbursement of the costs of drawing up the expert's opinion, the time limit for payment of the compensation - 30 days - and the conditions for bringing the parties to court. I will not discuss these issues in detail because of the very short speaking time. Amendment 4, to which Amendment 3 is tabled, gives a new wording to the article. 49. It shall include an authorisation for the Minister to issue a regulation setting out the manner and time limits for the declaration of injury, a detailed way of examining and assessing the damage, a detailed way of determining and paying compensation and a model protocol. Newly added article. 49a in turn refers to the scope not regulated by the Code of Administrative Procedure. Amendment 4 proposes amendments to the article. 50 and standardise the system for estimating damages and paying compensation and entrusting them to the voivodeship. Entries of Article. 46 provide for the liability of the State Treasury for any damage to crops and agricultural products caused by wild boars, elks, dwarfs and deer. So it is appropriate that all matters in this area be handled by a single authority - a voivode. Besides, this postulate has evolved in many opinions submitted to the bill. On the other hand, since the funds of the Compensation Fund come mainly from the contributions of the tenants or managers of the hunting circuit, this fund should be covered by the damage done in the areas of the hunting circuit, excluding damage caused by wild boar, elk, deer, dandelions and deer which are covered by the year-round protection. After all, it is not possible to cover damage from the tenants' contributions to hunting circuits, on which they have no impact. Amendments 4 and 5 refer to this problem. Amendment 6 introduces new articles, new regulations in Article. 50a and 50b. Art. 50a contains provisions on the creation of a Compensation Fund, which is a state-owned special-purpose fund. It provides that the detailed rules of the financial economy shall be defined by the Minister responsible for the environment in agreement with the Minister responsible for public finances. In turn, the article. 50b determines the method of calculation and transfer of contributions due to the fund. In Amendment 6, we propose setting the amount of the contribution based on the number of wild game included in the hunting plan, not in proportion to the area of hunting circuits, because this way of calculating them will allow better consideration of the different conditions of the hunting economy in different, less and more resourceful circuits. We're offering you a bet. [[♪ The bell ♪]] 600 zł per elk, 350 per deer, 135 per daniel, 105 per deer and 85 per wild boar. Marshal, I'm almost done.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u264	The Swine Poręba reservoir is built in Małopolska on the River Skawa, which is a tributary of the Vistula River. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u266	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the government draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws, the original print No 367. As a result of the long-term work of the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system, a document was drawn up on 28 April 2016 by the Committee on Public Finance on behalf of which I present the report in print No 464. The main objective of the proposed amendment is to introduce a tax avoidance clause in Polish legislation. The avoidance of taxation by some multinational corporations causes losses to the state budget. It is worth noting that these corporations, like their competitors operating only in the country, benefit equally from various public goods and services at their place of business. The avoidance of taxation by multinational corporations is a burden on the state budget and at the same time increases inequality. National companies have so far been put in a worse position. The tax collection audit presented by NIK at one of the meetings of the Public Finance Commission showed that the sums that were taken out of the country were many tens of billions of zlotys. The absence of a tax avoidance clause so far is an obstacle to ensuring the integrity of the Polish tax system. For introducing a tax avoidance clause in the Polish legal order, in accordance with Article. 84 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland speaks about the special protection of constitutional principles of tax justice, the universality of taxation and the protection of fiscal balance and the sustainability of public finances. The introduction of this clause should effectively discourage taxpayers from applying tax avoidance practices. The clause introduced will apply if measures with the characteristics of aggressive tax optimization result in a tax advantage exceeding 100 000. zł. The draft law provides for the creation of a Tax Avoidance Council which, as an independent opinion panel, will enhance predictability and a balanced way of applying the clause in individual cases. The scope of the proposed amendments is covered by the Law of 29 August 1997. Tax Order, Law of 30 August 2002. Law of the Court of Justice and the Law of 11 March 2004 on tax on goods and services. During the work of the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system, a number of amendments were tabled, a significant part of which was accepted in the subcommittee report and later in the committee. Amendments accepted by adding point 4a in Article 367 in print. 1, point 3 in Article. 3 and the new wording of the article. 7, the subcommittee considered it necessary because their adoption is an additional solution, which will further contribute to the sealing of the VAT system, which is particularly vulnerable to fraud. In the committee's report, in print No 464, these amendments are now: adding point 5 in Article. 1, point 2 in Article. 3 and amendment of the wording of Article. 9. Amendment in Article 1 consists of the imposition, as from 1 July 2016, of an obligation on large taxable persons keeping accounts using a computer to send by electronic means a single control file on VAT records, which has already been published on the website of the Ministry of Finance. In the amendments included in the committee's report, which are contained in the article. 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 9, it is proposed that from 1 January 2017, taxpayers should submit the above mentioned. a single control file supplemented by the tax identification number of the counterparties. From 1 January 2018, the obligation to send monthly electronic information on the VAT records would already cover all active VAT taxable persons. An interface software will be used to transfer files containing data, which will also support the transfer of single control file files to control purposes. The subcommittee also accepted the amendment to Article. 1 consisting of the addition of § Article 2 119b in the Tax Order Act. Former § 2 was deleted as part of the legislative amendments. The amendment adopted results from the need to allow for the application of Section IIIA, entitled "Avoidance of tax avoidance', in respect of the international facts covered by double taxation agreements. In view of the fact that the anti-tax avoidance clause contained in the Article. 119a of the draft law largely coincides with the grounds for applying the contractual clauses in question, it is justified to allow the application of the rules contained in Section IIIA of the draft law to those general clauses. This is precisely the case in view of the substantial extension of Section IIIA of the draft law, which also guarantees taxpayers much more protection than applying clauses directly from tax agreements. The planned centralisation of proceedings will involve the possibility of ensuring a high degree of professionalism by applying a clause against the avoidance of double taxation. The taxpayer will also be given the opportunity to consult the Anti-Tax Avoidance Council, which would not be possible for the application of contractual clauses by the field tax authorities. In addition to the print project No 367, the subcommittee added the current article. 4, thus amending Article. 29 in the Act of 10 September 2015 amending the Tax Order and certain other laws. The introduction of this amendment made it clear that the right to optional transfer during a transitional period, i.e. for 2 years, books and evidence in the form of a single control file by small and medium-sized enterprises also has the right to micro-entrepreneurs, because the micro-entrepreneur cannot be in a worse position than the small and medium-sized trader. The subcommittee also amended the current content of the Article. 7, in which the words of the actions carried out changed to those of the tax advantage obtained. This amendment will eliminate the interpretation doubts arising from the ambiguity of the operative event. The indication of a specific moment of action could be difficult to determine in practice. Currently, the term tax advantage within the meaning of the amended provisions refers to terms, i.e. terms to be defined in the Tax Order. The legal effect of the anti-avoidance clause is not just the existence of an artificial structure, but the existence of tax advantages as a result of it. Therefore, the subcommittee, when accepting government representatives, took these amendments into account in the subcommittee report, which was adopted at its meeting on 28 April 2016 by the Committee on Public Finance, and recommends that the High House adopt the report in print No 464. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u267	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Noblet, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u268	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the governmental draft law amending the Tax Order and certain other laws, prints No 367 and No 464. Following the first reading of the project at its 16th meeting of the Sejm on 13 April 2016, the project was directed to the Permanent Sub-Committee to monitor the tax system, where on 27 April this year. it was discussed in detail and adopted in conjunction with the amendments that the rapporteur spoke about. The main objective of the amendment is to introduce a tax avoidance clause in Polish legislation. Tax avoidance is a socially, morally and economically harmful phenomenon that occurs not only in Poland but also in many other countries. The experience of Poland and other countries so far shows that the double taxation agreements themselves are not sufficient to effectively prevent tax avoidance. It is worth noting that it was the European Commission that drew attention to the scale of the risks arising from tax avoidance. One of the solutions recommended by the Commission to counter aggressive tax planning practices resulting in tax avoidance is the adoption by Member States of a general rule on combating tax fraud. The European Parliament will stress, inter alia, that tax avoidance by certain multinational corporations can result in close zero effective tax rates for profits generated in European jurisdictions. Although these corporations use various public goods and services at their place of operation, they do not pay due taxes. Tax avoidance by legal persons has a direct impact on national budgets and on the distribution of fiscal effort between categories of taxpayers and between economic factors. The European Parliament concluded, on the basis of a study by the International Monetary Fund covering 51 countries, that the transfer of profits between tax jurisdictions results in an average reduction in revenue of around 5%. current income from corporate tax. In OECD countries, this decrease is almost 13%. In Poland, the Supreme Audit Chamber, in its findings of the audits contained in the "Surveillance of tax authorities and tax authorities on the regularity of settlements with the budget of the State of entities with a foreign capital', concluded that the absence of a clause against tax avoidance under applicable law constitutes an obstacle to ensuring the integrity of the Polish tax system. In view of the clear position of the Supreme Audit Chamber and the European institutions, the government has prepared an amendment to the Tax Order. The introduction of the anti-tax avoidance clause in the Polish legal order is supported by the special protection of constitutional principles of tax justice and universality and the protection of fiscal sustainability and sustainability of public finances. The introduction of the anti-tax avoidance clause into the Polish legal order will regulate the tax system. The anti-tax avoidance clause will apply if measures with the characteristics of aggressive tax optimization result in a tax advantage exceeding 100,000. zł. For symmetry, in order to minimise the consequences of the entry into force of the anti-tax avoidance clause, the Act proposes the introduction of safeguards to enable taxpayers to understand the position of the tax administration in relation to the planned or already executed transactions to which the clause may potentially apply. The Act provides for the creation of a Council for Anti-Tax Avoidance, [[♪ The bell ♪]] an independent body whose task is to assess the appropriateness of the application of the Article. 119a in individual cases.

These undefined phrases may be included in the legislation, especially if we are talking about a clause by which, in principle, all activities cannot be defined, but the Constitutional Tribunal has clearly indicated when such a clause can be allowed. Thus, firstly, the reasons for understanding a given undefined phrase must not leave too much room for individualized interpretation, secondly, it is necessary to give non-specific returns to the content guaranteeing uniformity of the case-lawline, thirdly, the interpretation of non-acute concepts must not lead to unlawful lawmaking by the law-abiding authorities. We are not dealing with all of this in the provisions on the clause. I have the impression that the Government of Law and Justice has translated the wording of the EU law clause directly to the living. Hence, we are talking about the subject matter and purpose of the Act and about artificial activities, i.e. legal concepts that do not exist in our system. How can the purpose of the bill be defined? That's what I was talking about. In EU law, tax laws generally have a preamble, and in this preamble it is precisely indicated what the objectives of the law are. With us, there is no such behaviour in relation to tax law, only and only justification for laws, and we know that laws can take a significantly different shape during legislative work. Hence, it will be very difficult to establish a uniform objective of the tax law. This means that taxpayers will only be subject to the interpretation of tax authorities. Therefore, we propose appropriate solutions in the amendments of the Modern Posel Club, introducing into the Tax Order a provision on the possibility of stopping the implementation of the decision covered by the application of the Article at the request of the taxpayer. 119a until the final settlement of the case by the administrative court. As regards the Anti-Tax Avoidance Council, we also call for two more members to be extended, i.e. the representative of the National Council of Legal Counsels and the National Bar Council. I share the Professor's position that art. 7 is in direct violation of the principle of non-retro non-agit lex, and this causes again the non-constitutionality of the rule, the infringement of the article. 2 Constitutional Act, [[♪ The bell ♪]] the principles of a democratic rule of law. The Modern Posel Club will support the solutions submitted subject to the adoption of the amendments tabled by the Modern Club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u279	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, a club of the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u280	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position to the committee's report on the government draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws, print No 367 and No 464. The bill concerns a new instrument, a new tax system tool, which is the anti-tax avoidance clause. This instrument operates in many countries around the world, it can be said to be a fairly common instrument that has been used for many years, just as the very negative phenomenon of seeking to pay the lowest taxes or to avoid tax at all is common. This negative phenomenon of tax avoidance occurs in every market economy country. The anti-tax avoidance clause has already functioned in our country, but very briefly. The Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment that it was incompatible with the Article. 2 in conjunction with Article. 217 constitutions. The Court found that the regulation introduced at that time did not comply with the constitutional standards of decent legislation and consequently violated the fundamental elements forming the substance of the principle of trust in the State and the law in question. The desirable features to which the Court drew attention in its decision were above all the definition and predictability. We have doubts as to whether the current law meets these requirements laid down by the Court. It follows from the definition of the tax avoidance clause that this is an act carried out in order to achieve a tax advantage which, in the circumstances, is contrary to the object and purpose of the Tax Act and the mode of action of the taxable person was artificial. Already by definition, it is possible to read out the wide-ranging flexibility which the control services may have to rely on in order to justify the taxpayer's artificial activities. On the other hand, we welcome the provision that the tax avoidance clause will apply to large transactions where the tax advantage will exceed 100,000. zł. The effect of the clause will above all be the possibility for tax authorities to recover unpaid taxes, as well as its preventive role must be underlined. Taxpayers are aware of the risks and consequences arising from the correction of the accounts: payment of the tax due together with interest even in the absence of any other financial sanctions. The creation of a Council for Anti-Tax Avoidance, an opinion-based institution, which will assess the appropriateness of applying the clause in individual cases, should be welcomed. It will therefore be the subject of supervision over the application of the clause and the opinion will be issued at the request of the Minister of Finance if the taxpayer so requests. The introduction of the anti-tax avoidance clause also raises many doubts and can be regarded as an attack on the rights of the taxpayer and the freedom to shape the content of contracts. A number of substantive and financial considerations are raised by the introduction of the so-called. a protective opinion issued by the Minister of Finance at the request of the taxpayer in order to determine whether the action envisaged or carried out by the person concerned is tax avoidance. The time limit for such a decision, which is set without undue delay, but extended up to 6 months, and a fee of 20,000, raises the most doubts. zł. This fee is too high and therefore it simply seems to be a deterrent in the application for a safeguard opinion. In summary, this project, although there is some doubt, is of a sealing nature of the tax system. It is used to combat tax evasion, which occurs on a large scale, so introducing it into a realistic competitive environment for honest entrepreneurs. The final decision of my club of the Polish People's Party will be made [[♪ The bell ♪]] when assessing the final version of this project. Thank you.

It follows from the very essence of the anti-tax avoidance clause that it applies to structures with a high level of complexity, which consist of many operations and elements. As proposed in the original draft, the wording of the Article. 119f § 1 also means a combination of related activities performed by the same or different entities. Therefore, it could be difficult to identify a specific point of action. For example, if an element of the optimization structure would be the payment of dividends to a Polish company by a foreign company established in the European Union - such a dividend, I would remind you, may be wholly exempt under the Parent-Subsidiary Directive - it would be difficult to clearly determine whether the activity related to this optimisation scheme was the creation of a foreign company or any payment of dividends. If it were considered that the moment of the operation was the establishment of a foreign company, the provisions of the anti-tax avoidance clause could in fact not apply in this case. The aim is to enhance the protection of workers' rights and to prevent illegal employment. On 31 March, the Sejm held its first reading of the draft law, and then the Marshal of the Sejm sent the draft to the Extraordinary Commission on changes in codification, and this, in turn, addressed the draft to the Permanent Sub-Committee on the revision of the Labour Code. During the work of the sub-committee, one amendment was adopted, replacing the modal verb with a statement stating strongly that the employer confirms the worker's written findings before he was admitted to work. During the examination of the subcommittee report on 27 April, the committee accepted one more amendment to the draft concerning the entry into force of the Act. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u311	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Please, Mrs Barbara Bartus, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u312	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I was also honored to present my Parliamentary Club's position on the report of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Labour Code Act in print No 452. Art. 1 of the proposed law does not change the principle currently expressed in the first sentence of Article. 29 § 2 of the Labour Code, according to which the employment contract should be concluded in writing. Nor does it modify the principle that a contract of employment concluded without a written form shall remain valid and, in the event of a dispute, the evidence of witnesses and the hearing of the parties shall be admissible. The draft only provides for a modification of the moment at which the staff member should be confirmed with regard to the parties to the contract, the type of contract and its terms and conditions. The change assumes that this must be done before the employee is admitted to work. In accordance with the provisions in force, the date of written confirmation of the employment contract is the day of commencement of work, and on the basis of Article. Article 36 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 Act on the social security system the application to the Social Security Office may be made within 7 days of the employment of the employee. The experience of labour inspectors shows that many employers do not keep these deadlines. The disclosure of such facts of undeclared work by labour inspectors usually results in an employer's finding that the person disclosed in the course of the audit has been working since today, and therefore the deadlines for confirmation in writing of the terms of the employment contract and notification to the Social Security Office have not yet expired. You could say that in this way the so-called. The first day of work for many employees lasts for weeks, months, and sometimes for years. Every day is the first day at work, of course in the case of labour inspection. In fact, the current 7-day time limit for reporting a person to social security and the obligation to conclude a contract of employment at the latest on the day of the start of work make it possible to conduct black employment. Most people involved in undeclared work are convinced of the low effectiveness of the control activities of state institutions and therefore live in a sense of the absence of any threat from these institutions, because it can always be explained. Such a practice has even gained its name - it is so-called. First-day syndrome. During the committee meeting there was an interesting and lively discussion on sanctions concerning this legal standard. Such sanctions for offences against workers' rights are provided for in Section 13 of the Labour Code. This is a penalty of 1 000. PLN up to 30,000. zł. The amendment also takes account of the amendment of the article. 281 § 2 of the Labour Code to the extent necessary. However, in order for a sanction to be applied, the disposition of the legal standard must be sufficiently precise to prevent circumvention, and this is the reason for the proposed amendment to the Labour Code. Building a belief in the inevitability of a penalty should be a factor in the legalisation of employment. It is therefore reasonable for the legislator to require that written confirmation be given as to the nature and conditions of the employment contract before the worker is admitted to work. Finally, I would like to stress that the draft and the two amendments introduced later have a positive recommendation from the Council of Social Dialogue, namely the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy and the entire government of Prime Minister Beata Szydło works not only quickly and efficiently, but also in dialogue with the social side, not against it. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this revision of the Labour Code and vote in favour of the proposed amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u337	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Bożena Borys-Raccoon, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u338	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Seym! I would like to present to the High House the position of the Law and Justice Club vis-à-vis the Government's draft law amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws, print No 382, committee report - print No 453. The draft of this law is to bring Polish labour law provisions into line with Directive 2006/54/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 5 July 2006 aimed at introducing the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment of men and women in employment and adapting authorisations to Article. 92. 1st Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Current wording of the article. 176 The Labour Code states that women must not be employed for work that is particularly burdensome or harmful to health. This provision also establishes a delegation to the Council of Ministers to establish a list of these work in the Regulation. The European Commission has found the provisions of that provision incompatible with the provisions of Directive 2002/77/EC on the introduction of the principle of equal treatment for men and women as regards access to employment, vocational training and promotion and working conditions. The current article. 176 The Labour Code applies to all women, not just pregnant and breastfeeding women. In the Commission's view, this record is an obstacle to ensuring gender equality. The draft amendment therefore envisages a revision of the current wording of the article. 176 The Labour Code, according to which a special form of protection will apply only to pregnant and breastfeeding women who, according to the amended provision, will not be able to carry out work which is burdensome, dangerous or harmful to health, which may adversely affect the health of pregnant women, the course of pregnancy or the feeding of the child. Proposed amendments to the Article. 176 In addition, it will be necessary to amend the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and the Act on the military service of professional soldiers. In both of these laws, amendments will concern the prohibition on the performance of duties, functions or duties which are dangerous, harmful or burdensome to health, by pregnant women and during breast-feeding. It must be noted here that the European Commission's reservations were raised in 2014. The previous Minister of Labour has not taken any action for almost two years to make the necessary changes to the Labour Code and to bring these provisions into line with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the recommendations of the European Commission, which today's constitutional defenders seem to have forgotten. Thanking the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy for another sign of good change, the Law and Justice Club welcomes the draft law on amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws, print No 382, and supports the proposed amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u346	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I am pleased to present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws, prints No. 382 and 453. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed amendment is based on clarification of the Article. 176 The Labour Code, which states that work which is burdensome, dangerous or harmful to health cannot be performed by pregnant women and breast-feeding children. The current rules have been challenged by the European Commission. According to the Commission, in its Article. 176 The Labour Code as it stands so far shows that it applies to all women, and this in turn conflicts with the idea of gender equality in access to employment. We have carefully examined the justification for the draft novel and Directive 2006/54/EC of the European Parliament on equality between women and men in the labour market. We fully agree that equality between men and women is one of the fundamental principles of European Union law. It applies to all aspects of social life, including employment. The novel also rightly provides for a new list of work that can have an adverse effect on women's health, pregnancy or breast-feeding. Both laws will include prohibitions on soldiers - women's duties, duties or duties which are dangerous, harmful or burdensome to health during pregnancy or breastfeeding.
