ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u351	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed to the examination of item 15. of the agenda: First reading of the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the law Family and caring code and some other laws (print No 448). I ask the Secretary of State at the Office of the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Dera, to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u352	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of President Andrzej Duda, I have the honour to present a bill amending the Family and Care Code Act and some other laws. This project is an expression of the President's concern that the 500+ funds actually go to families, to support families in raising children. It's a project that doesn't directly touch the bill. I call it a shielding project that makes it possible for the 500+ law to function smoothly and to reach the recipients. On 1 April, the Law of 11 February on State aid to raise children entered into force. Its essence is the financial support of families with dependent children up to the age of 18. The Act introduces a new monetary benefit into the legal system, a parenting benefit allowing families to reduce the financial burden of raising children. This provision is a long-term instrument of family policy and pursues three main objectives. Firstly, it encourages decision-making about having more offspring and thus improving the remaining in Poland for years at a level that has not been guaranteed so-called. the simple replacementability of generations of the fertility rate. Secondly, it reduces the risk of poverty, especially among the youngest Poles. And thirdly, it is an investment in human capital that is the basis for building a strong state. According to Art. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 The purpose of this law is to partially cover the costs of raising a child, including the care and care of the child's needs. This benefit is therefore a maintenance function. The nature of the benefit also indicates the content of the justification for the draft law, which shows that the educational provision is directed to families with dependent children and is intended primarily to cover part of the expenses related to meeting the needs of life and raising children. Education and preparation for the life of children entails a high financial burden for dependants, in particular in multi-member families. As a result, families often face economic barriers related to the size of their income. It is therefore necessary to introduce solutions to eliminate or significantly reduce them. This law will reduce the current economic constraints, especially among young people who decide to have children, especially the second and the next child. This is the justification for the bill adopted by the High House. The above in comparison with the circumstances... The Act does not explicitly determine the relationship between parenting and maintenance obligations. In practice, this may raise doubts as to whether such financial support should be taken into account when determining the scope of the maintenance obligation. Here, the statements of the legal doctrine on this problem are few, for obvious reasons, and quite general, but two different positions can already be observed. In accordance with one of these positions, the parental benefit should be taken into account by the family courts in determining the amount of maintenance. This is due to the absence of a provision that the benefit does not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation. Moreover, although State aid does not exempt parents from maintenance obligations, the family court must take into account all the benefits that the child receives in determining the legitimate needs of the child and the material situation of the parents. That's one theory in doctrine. The second says - this is a completely different doctrine in turn, a different view - that family courts, specifying the scope of the maintenance obligation, should be abstracted from the educational provision. Parents' commitment to their children is fundamental and impossible exemption from this obligation, and therefore a reduction in the amount of maintenance on account of the entitlement to provide supplementary public-funded education would be unlawful. It could be said that so far this dispute is only going on on the ground of legal doctrine, but practice, as always, shows in fact, whether it's just a theory or it's becoming something else. Well, on April 21st, according to the media, we had the first such case in Poland, that the court did not judge the maintenance amount requested by the child's mother, because it thought that she could try to obtain a benefit under the 500+ programme. So clearly, the judge here was in favour of the first of these views, for the first doctrine. High Izbo! In this respect, however, it is necessary to act at this point so that the dispute does not run in court, only that there are clear laws which will resolve the dispute, so that there is no dispute here, that we do not have such a situation that some courts will walk one line at a time, another at a time, then it will go to the Supreme Court and we will wait for the ruling of the Supreme Court to establish a line of inquiry. The President believes that this dispute must be cut at this level and that the changes should be clearly recorded so that this dispute simply does not exist here. In this position, the President proposed a change in the article to put an end to this dispute. 135 § 3 of the Family and Care Code, which regulates the impact of social benefits on the existence and scope of maintenance obligations. This change consists in a clear indication that the parental benefit and its equivalent allowance in the amount of the parental benefit falling within the category of benefits and other cash for children placed in the foster care indicated in the proposed article. § 135 § 3 point 2 of the Family and Care Code, do not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation. This will avoid possible discrepancies in the case law of the family courts and ensure that the provisions of the Law of 11 February 2016 on State aid to raise children are applied in accordance with the intention of the legislator. In the view of the President, it cannot be used to reduce maintenance burdens and to help those obliged to do so, especially in a situation where maintenance is so low. This would not only contradict its essence, but would also grossly violate the principle of social justice. What you are proposing to the President is in line with the case law of the Constitutional Court, which has spoken on this matter and is also in line with the Convention on the Rights of the Child. Art. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Convention states that it is the responsibility of the States to take all appropriate steps to ensure that parents or other persons with financial responsibility for the child provide for the child. Thus, as is the case in the doctrine, the State, acting in pursuit of the right of a child to a decent living, should first of all force the responsible behaviour of his parents towards the child, in order to avoid the situation in which social law or the practice of applying the law would lead to the erosion of maintenance obligations. The support of parents by the State to provide the child with means of subsistence cannot be understood as the service of parents and the transfer of burdens to taxpayers. It is hard to imagine a clearer view. This change is going precisely in this direction, so that there is no dispute about it. Although we have such records that we have a convention, practice shows that this can be interpreted differently. And in line with these positions and in view of the ratio legis of the Law on State aid to raise children, which is to improve the situation of families raising children, it is necessary, in the view of the President, to amend the proposed bill. It will ensure that this law is applied in accordance with the intention of the legislator to adopt it without the need to seek a targeted interpretation in this regard. At the same time, it is also appropriate to regulate the impact on the scope of maintenance obligations other than the provision of education and an allowance in the amount of educational benefits intended to cover the cost of child care for parents or guardians of children, family homes and institutional foster care. In the light of the current wording of the article. Article 135 (3) of the Family and Care Code does not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation by providing social assistance or maintenance fund referred to in the Law of 7 September 2007 on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance to be reimbursed by the maintenance obligation and the provision to the foster family. The legislator did not include this provision, among others. family benefits as defined in the Law of 28 November 2003 on family benefits. However, these benefits are identical to educational benefits. The provision of education was modelled on the provision of family benefits. In this case, therefore, the above arguments for not including them in determining the scope of the maintenance obligation remain valid. The Supreme Court has already confirmed this position with regard to the family allowance in 1967, so that there is no more doubt here. According to the wording of the explanatory memorandum of the Supreme Court's resolution: When judging a child's child's entitlement to child support, the court does not take into account the family allowance. This allowance is for the needs of the child regardless of the child's statutory maintenance. The above supports the extension of the scope of this Article. 135 § 3 in the Family and Care Code. On the other hand, with regard to the welfare benefits of children placed in foster care, it should be pointed out that the scope of the maintenance obligation is currently not affected solely by the benefit to the foster family. In the contents of this article. Article 135 (3) of the Family and Care Code does not include benefits granted to persons who run the family's homes and to the institution's foster care. The President's view is that there is no justification for the different treatment of children's benefits for the maintenance of various forms of foster care, and therefore the draft law proposes to supplement the list of benefits which do not affect the scope of the maintenance obligation also on the maintenance of children received by family homes and on the basis of institutional foster care. High Izbo! The third of the proposed amendments contained in the draft law concerns the exclusion of amounts not subject to enforcement on the basis of a judicial or administrative implementing title, including a parental benefit and an allowance in the amount of a parental benefit, from a bail-out from a bank account. It should be pointed out here that it has been introduced in Article. 31 The Law of 11 February 2016 on State aid to raise children, amending Article 31 of the Act of 11 February 2016 on State aid to raise children. 833 in the Code of Civil Procedure, aimed at excluding the amount of parental benefit and benefits, allowances and other amounts enumeratively listed in the provisions of the Law on Family Support and foster care from enforcement, will not be effective in the execution of the bank account. This type of enforcement of claims is characterised by a specific feature of the fact that the cash that affects the bank account loses the quality of its origin, loses its identity and becomes merely the funds collected in the account. Thus, even if they are protected from execution under specific rules, e.g. part of the remuneration for work, at the moment of impact on the bank account, shall be executed in accordance with the rules laid down for the execution of claims from the bank account. The executing authority, i.e. the judicial bailiff or any other enforcement body, has no knowledge of what funds are in the occupied bank account and where they come from. Thus, in the current legal system, debtors granted e.g. a parent's benefit or an allowance in the amount of a parent's benefit, in order to avoid their transfer to the amounts due, should apply for a limitation of the execution from the bank account by the amount derived from the benefit or allowance. The submission of such a request involves the obligation to show the origin of such amounts, which should not be too difficult because of the specific amount of the benefit and the title of the transfer, but will require additional formalities. And again, we have a situation in which life has confirmed the foresight of the President that this needs to be changed. We have recently learned, when this project was already submitted in the Sejm, that it happened a few days ago to a curiosity and unusual situation in the municipality of Puck. According to the media, the mother of six children from Puck came to collect her due money - 3,000. zł. The mother of six children. When the woman stood by the post office window in Puck, it turned out that the account had been cleared. In the presence of her children, a shook up mom slipped to the floor. This was not what it was supposed to look like last week - said Mrs.Sylwia Dawidowska, the mother of these children. It's supposed to be guaranteed, and the bailiff won't touch it. In this case it was different - the bailiff of Szczecin mercilessly removed money from the account from the program Why? Because this lady in October last year received a notification of the bailiff's execution on her bank account - an employee of the firm is explained. "Then she should write us a letter saying that there are family benefits. Exactly what I just read about is confirmed. It is difficult to require all Polish residents to know the law so that they know that it is their duty to send a letter, inform the bailiff. Unfortunately, the level of legal awareness of Poles is very low. The laws that you propose to the President are about to free citizens from being asked to do so, so that it is by law, so that it is simply ex lege, that these measures are simply secured and not to be moved for bailiffs. And in order to avoid precisely such situations as I mentioned just moments ago, described by the media, this change is being introduced to exclude funds not subject to execution from the bank account under Article. 833 § 6 and 7 of the Law of 17 November 1964. Code of Civil Procedure. It is therefore proposed to add in Article. 890 Code of Civil Procedure provision excluding benefits referred to in Article. 833 § 6 and 7 from the execution of the bank account. The attachment of the claim from this account will therefore not include: maintenance benefits, cash benefits paid in the event of inefficiencies in the execution of maintenance, family benefits, family allowances, care, childbirth, for full orphans, welfare benefits, social assistance benefits, integration benefits, but also educational benefits and benefits, allowances and other amounts referred to in Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, Article 80, Art. 81, Art. 83, Art. 84 and Article 84 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). 140 Law on support for family and foster care, and financial resources for the maintenance of housing in a multi-family building or single-family house referred to in Article. Paragraph 83. Article 2 and Article 2 Article 84 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Act on support of family and foster care system. Analogous regulation regarding the release from bank account execution is also proposed by the President in the article. 54 of the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law. Such a solution will provide effective protection against the execution of claims covered by the enforcement title of benefits released from executions which were held in a bank account at the time of the seizure or have affected that account after the seizure has taken place. The introduction of this regulation, in the opinion of the President, will prevent the bank from carrying out bail-out benefits released from execution due to their exclusion from this class. This appropriation will not be used to cover the enforcement of the claim. The bank's task will be to notify the bailiff that part of the amount on the bank account cannot be transferred because it comes from non-occupied benefits. At the same time, it does not appear that the bank's determination of the source of the funds held in the client's bank account entails any difficulties, that the bank, having full access to the client's account, should have no problem establishing the titles on which the client received certain funds. In the view of the President, the adoption of this project, firstly, will cause the measures that you have committed to support families to reach these families, secondly, to make life easier for citizens, because they will not have to come forward with proposals, as they do at the moment, and will not be surprised at the fact that money is suddenly disappearing and will not be experiencing a kind of trauma at this point. As I said at the outset, this project is due to the concern that these funds - public funds, State budget resources - do not disappear along the way to reach those who need it most. Hence the hot appeal for support of this bill, for the adoption of this law as soon as possible so that the situations described recently by the media do not take place any more. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u394	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The establishment of the Academy of War Art involves the need to make profound structural changes in the present National Defence Academy and has been preceded by a longer period of reflection and study work preparing these changes. Please note that, before we went to propose this law to the High House, it was almost 4.5 months. During this time, we first collected opinions about universities in both military and academic environments, then ordered two audits: the first of the officers of the General Staff who carried out such an analysis on my behalf, and the second audit carried out by an independent consulting firm who carried out this assessment and actually presented the report. It was only on the basis of these studies that we decided to present this proposal to the High House in the form of a bill. I would like to make it clear that the university, first of all, must be, in working language, enlisted, which is strongly linked to the army and its needs and in this direction... so it is necessary to adapt its research and teaching offer. The situation, as we all know, is very variable, changing very quickly, dynamically, poses completely new challenges for us. The establishment of the Academy of War Art based on the existing structure of the Academy of National Defence will complement and extend its existing teaching, scientific and research activities. We don't want to give up anything, I mean. The last thing, I mean. increase the role and competence of the scientific and expert facilities. We are fully aware that in Poland there are at the moment many centres that conduct ongoing analyses of the situation both military and economic and political in various regions of the world. But the army wants to have its own centre, which will give these analyses in terms of military challenges, and at the same time, if there is another centre, competition will only help to increase the level of these analyses and increase the level of work carried out there. We want to make it very clear that these centres, which we will create and support, based on subsidies from the national defence department, are to meet the highest quality criteria and be confronted also with foreign centres. Here the competition itself, competition from Polish centers, as we think, is not enough. This is another element that requires a profound reform and a very clear impulse. And such a purpose was also reflected in the draft and justification of this law, which I ask the High House to adopt. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Several military universities have been wound up, some have been merged into one, but they have operated until 2005 under a separate military education system as defined by the Military Higher Education Act. And during this period, because of the lack of training of officers or at least the absence of contracts for such training, these universities became more and more civil universities. The Academy of National Defence was established in 1990 by the merger of the Academy of General Staff and a certain part of the Military Political Academy. These were two central military universities in the times of the Polish People's Republic. The Academy has maintained certain functions related to the needs of the military, in particular in the field of training and upgrading the qualifications of senior military officers. At present, however, at the Academy of National Defence, about 8,000 are educated in 10 Bachelor's degrees and 7 Master's degrees. purely civilian students. The specificity of academic institutions makes such changes take place over the next few years. Young people who are currently studying there will study and finish their studies in the current fields of study. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u450	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! I have the honour to present to the High House a bill amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act and some other laws. The aim of the proposed law is to increase the transparency of the criminal process by strengthening the constitutional principle of transparency of the hearing in criminal proceedings and by linking the possibility for representatives of the mass media to record and transmit the hearing with the transparency of its conduct. To this end, the proposed law limits the scope of the admissible exemptions to the disclosure of the hearing on the basis of a condition of important private interest, introduces the possibility of challenging the court's decision on the disclosure of the hearing and excludes the possibility of a priori refusal to allow the proceedings to be established and transmitted by media representatives. High Izbo! In the Polish legal system, transparency of the hearing is a fundamental constitutional principle. It shall be applicable in all judicial proceedings. Constitution of the Republic of Poland in art. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 only in enumerative cases allows exceptions to the principle of transparency, i.e. for reasons of morality, state security, public order and the protection of private life or other important private interest. The principle of transparency qualifies as an element of the right to court, i.e. the right of an individual to a fair and public trial in which its rights are settled or in which criminal charges are brought against it. In its external dimension, which includes the possibility of taking part in the hearing of the public, the principle of transparency is intended to ensure the verification of the external compliance with the law of the court and to ensure the exercise of the rights of the parties involved in the proceedings and thus constitutes one of the guarantors of the integrity of the process. Publicity of the case also promotes the presentation of the right attitude of the judge vis-à-vis the parties involved in the proceedings, counters the establishment of incorrect ones, because they do not respect the seriousness of the justice system and the dignity of the judicial office, relations with the participants in the proceedings, favoring or discriminate against one of the parties and thus, High Izbo, shapes the trust and positive image of the justice system in society. In view of the above, exceptions to the principle of transparency should be interpreted restrictively, i.e. to the extent necessary for the implementation of the principles of a democratic rule of law. If it's the organist in the mouth. Article 2 45 of the Constitution has merely indicated the possibility of excluding the transparency of the case in the enumerative cases mentioned, which means that the ordinary legislature has the right, but not the obligation, to transfer the exemptions of disclosure to the level of regulation of specific procedures laid down therein. This is also confirmed by the existing wording § Article 1 360 of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which contains only a catalogue of permitted exemptions from the principle of transparency of the criminal process. The State's security and public policy objective is broadly consistent with the content of the condition set out in point 3 (1) of Article. 360 k.p.k. - the threat of disclosure of circumstances which, on account of the important interest of the State, should be kept secret, and in paragraph 1 § Article 1 360 k.p.k. - the threat of disturbing public peace. The constitutional condition for the admissibility of the exclusion of disclosure for reasons of morality corresponds, in substance, to the criminal procedural condition indicated in point 2 § Article 1 360 k.p.k., as well as the reason for the threat of an important private interest, is paragraph 4 § Article 1 360 k.p.k. However, in the current version of the Criminal Procedure Act, the grounds for the protection of the private life of the parties, which in the catalogue of constitutionally permissible restrictions of disclosure, constitute a condition separate from that of an important private interest, have been omitted. In the legislator's view, in the context of criminal proceedings, the unconditional protection of the constitutional principle of transparency and therefore the fairness of the case should be strengthened by limiting the possibility of excluding disclosure on the basis of an important private interest only in cases where an important private interest of the victim, a witness or a non-Participant would be threatened. On the other hand, as regards the protection of the defendant's private interest, it is justified to give such consent on the basis of the opposite party's position and, if a prosecutor is involved, who is also an advocate of the rule of law, the prosecutor's consent should be required. The aim of this solution is to prevent abuse of this basis of exclusion of disclosure for reasons which are directly in the interest of the person who has been accused of the offence. The legislator assumes that there is no axiological justification for excluding the disclosure of the hearing due to any private interest of the accused, and that the indeterminateness of this condition constitutes an area of abuse, thus undermining confidence in the justice system and destructively affecting the image of justice in society. Furthermore, in the light of the nature of the changes in procedural rules in question, the legislator believes that the amendment of the Article should be considered justified. 181 k.p.k. with regard to the determination of the reasons which will not result in a hearing to be held, except in the case of disclosure. The legislator does not see the justification in the automatic exclusion of transparency of the hearing. High Izbo! Furthermore, the introduction of controls on the so-called "criminal process" will be a guarantee of strengthening the transparency of the criminal process. in the case of a horizontal order on the disclosure of the court's case, and in the light of the need to hear the case without undue delay, the proposed solution provides for a limitation of the period of time for which the complaint is to be heard up to 7 days from the date of referral to the court of the complaint together with the necessary files. Another proposed measure to strengthen the transparency of the criminal process is to ensure social control over the regularity of the proceedings by extending the principle of the admissibility of the recording and transmission by the media of the hearing, for which no provision has been made to conduct it except for transparency. Only in the event of a disturbance in the order of the hearing by the representatives of the media will the court be entitled to order that some of them leave the Chamber. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 357 § 1 k.p.k. In the current wording, the court's authorisation to consolidate the image and sound from the course of the hearing makes it conditional on the occurrence of inconsequential reasons, which make the decision on the matter in its indisputability often arbitrary. In the reality of the information society, the consolidation and transmission of the hearing by representatives of the mass media are part of the transparency of the hearing and also serve to inform the public about the process - thus performing the function of transparency of the hearing, i.e. checking the accuracy and regularity of the process - compliance with the procedural guarantees of its participants and shaping legal attitudes and legal awareness of society. Ex definitione is therefore in favour of a legitimate social interest. In view of the fact that the recording and transmission of the hearing are part of its transparency, it is also unjustified to maintain the admissibility of media participation depending on whether the participant's important interests do not object to it. The protection of an important private interest of a party to proceedings is guaranteed to the extent necessary by the institution to exclude the transparency of the hearing, which is Article. 360 k.p.k. In view of the above, it is not justified to maintain the admissibility of the limitation of the transparency of the hearing as regards media participation in the current form. Given that it cannot be ruled out that, given the considerable public interest in the process, the presence of a large number of representatives of the media in the Chamber may hinder its conduct, e.g. for technical, organisational or order reasons, the proposed legal solution provides for the possibility for the court to order certain of them to leave the courtroom and, in particularly justified cases, by all persons. The aim of the project is also to broaden the scope for action in the social factor process in the form of a social representative. This factor, in particular, enables social control of justice and the presentation to courts of positions of a social organisation, which undoubtedly creates better conditions for a fair judgment on the case. It is therefore necessary to remove unnecessary barriers to the participation of the social representative in the criminal process and to ensure that he is fully represented by the position of the social organisation. To this end, it was first of all necessary to eliminate the arbitraryness of the courts in allowing a representative of the social organisation to participate in the case. Such authorisation should always be mandatory if at least one of the parties agrees to do so. The participation of a social representative is therefore to be subject to the agreement of at least one of the parties, which is justified by the fact that he is to represent an interest relating to the subject matter of the dispute between those parties. The Party may withdraw its consent at any time. If the parties do not agree to the participation of the social representative, the court shall exclude that representative from the participation in the case. The implementation of the interests of justice in relation to the participation of a social representative is to be ensured that, regardless of the views of the parties, the court can, on account of the important interests of the judiciary, allow the representative of such an organisation. The refusal to allow a representative of a social organisation may take place only if the social or individual interest indicated in the notification to the social organisation is not covered by the statutory tasks of that organisation or is not related to the case being recognised. As only formal questions can be examined by the court in allowing the social representative to participate in the case, it may happen that due to many of those who submit their participation, the proper course of proceedings will not be ensured. The Court of First Instance will then be able to limit the number of representatives of social organisations present in the case. The prosecutor and the accused will then be able to identify no more than two representatives of the social organisations who will be involved in the case, and if there is more than one accused or one prosecutor, each of them will be able to identify one representative. The refusal of a representative shall be deemed to withdraw his consent to appear on the case, with consequences in the proposed Article. 90 § 3 k.p.k. In addition, the draft provides for a mandatory and not only where necessary, giving a voice to the social representative before the judgment is delivered. It's an art. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day In view of the fact that the social representative is not a party to the proceedings, it has become necessary to amend this chapter, Chapter 46, and other provisions which regulated the parties' voices. According to the draft, a social representative who is not a party will take the floor. It is therefore proposed that the term "final votes' be adopted. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 2 of the draft is also amended as regards Article. 133 k.p.k., which aims to facilitate professional representatives of criminal proceedings. The provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure provide for a special method of service for procedural purposes - Chapter 15 of the Code of Criminal Procedure. - which constitutes a comprehensive regulation of its standardization, specific to the provisions of the Postal Law of 23 November 2012. This last law allows, among other things, to. issue with effect of service of the consignment to the postal office of the addressee authorised on the basis of a postal proxy - Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 2 of the Postal Law - which is a convenient and widely used way of receiving consignments, especially by professional operators. The rules of the procedural law do not provide for such a way of receiving postal items, in particular in order to ensure that those consignments are delivered directly to the hands of addressees who are non-professional participants in criminal proceedings. Therefore, this means of receiving consignments cannot currently be used. This restriction appears to be justified for non-professional participants, since a postal proxy can be given to any person - Article. Article 38 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Postal law - and the adoption of another solution in practice would result in a reduction in the actual efficiency of service and in many situations real doubts as to the status of informing the participant of its powers and procedural obligations. These problems do not arise in relation to professional participants in the proceedings: lawyers and legal advisers who are engaged in their professions in law firms. For this reason, in order to improve and facilitate the receipt of letters addressed to professional participants - i.e. defenders or attorneys who are lawyers or legal advisers - left in post offices, the drafter proposes to add an article. 133 § 2a k.p.k., allowing for this to be done by persons authorised on the basis of a postal proxy to receive mail. It should be noted that a similar regulation providing for the receipt of letters by persons authorised on the basis of a postal proxy has been introduced in Article. 139 § 11 c.p.c. Act amending of 16 September 2011 amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act and certain other laws. The amendments to the Code of Criminal Procedure are supplemented by amendments to the Article. 40 Law of 5 December 1999 on the professions of a doctor and dentist regulating medical confidentiality. In the current legal state, the scope of medical confidentiality is too broad, which often makes it impossible for people closest to a deceased patient to pursue claims or liability towards persons who may have contributed to the death of the patient. As a result, medical confidentiality can be used in individual cases to hide errors in treatment against the interests of the patient. Disclosure of objective causes of death may also be in the public interest. The introduction of the design will enable the immediate person to know all the circumstances related to the patient's health and may precede the decision to notify the suspect of the crime. As a result, in many cases, this may also avoid the need to initiate unnecessary proceedings, as disclosure alone will be sufficient for the loved ones to make good the harm if they contribute to the patient's death. The subject of this mystery after the patient's death should be those closest to the patient who has linked a node of special trust to the patient so that they are not unduly affected by the patient's personal well-being. The definition of a loved one is defined in Article. 3 (1) (a) 1 point 2 of the Act of 6 November 2008 on the Rights of the Patient and the Ombudsperson on the Rights of the Patient, to which the Act on Doctors refers in Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6. It should also have the right to object to the exemption from secrecy where they consider disclosure of confidential information to constitute a disproportionate and unjustified breach of personal property. In such a case, it is sufficient to object to the confidentiality of medical confidentiality to one of the persons close to the circle of persons specified in the Act, even though other loved ones may consent to the disclosure of information. With an amendment to the article. 40 Act on Competitions of Doctor and Dentist correlates the amendment to Article. 14 of the Act of 6 November 2008 on the Rights of the Patient and the Ombudsperson of the Rights of the Patient. This provision is a functional complement to this horizontal act, equivalent to amendments to the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist. As a result of the entry into force of the law in criminal proceedings carried out at the stage of the hearing of the court, there may be a need to change subjectively and in the present case. This may apply to proceedings initiated both before 1 July 2015, after that date and after 15 April 2016, i.e. proceedings under different rules of procedure for evidence. As a result of the partial repeal of medical confidentiality or the intervention of a social organisation, new circumstances may arise, the need to seek new evidence, or even a reasonable suspicion that the evidence collected contains significant deficiencies which need to be completed in the preparatory proceedings. If these deficiencies are limited, they may be supplemented at the hearing, and if they are significantly broader, there are currently no provisions allowing them to be effectively supplemented without excessive extension of judicial proceedings. For this reason, the draft proposes a provision of an incidental nature allowing for the return to the preliminary proceedings of any case in which the public prosecutor has raised doubts as to its substantive correctness. On the other hand, the proposed amendment goes beyond these standards, and this goes beyond dangerously. It is established as a principle of the presence of media in the courtroom, without the prior consent of the court. Only in very specific cases will the court be able to order a media representative to leave the courtroom. However, a much greater concern is raised by the proposed change as regards the exclusion of the transparency of the procedure itself. In the event that there is even a very real risk that disclosure of the trial violates the important interests of the accused, that is, a person who is not yet convicted, the exclusion of disclosure will nevertheless only be possible with the consent of the prosecutor if he participates in the proceedings. Thus, the legal inequality of the parties to the trial is introduced and the powers of the judges, who are responsible for the criminal process, are significantly reduced. It is worth recalling here that since 4 March 2016, the entire prosecution is subject to only one politician, Minister Zbigniew Ziobra. It is therefore reasonable to fear that prosecutors' decisions in this area may often be of a non-essential nature. The proposed changes to the exclusion of transparency and access to the media are unfortunately the easiest way to create conditions for public participation. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u470	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, it is my pleasure to present our position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure and some other laws contained in print No.451. Closer analysis of the regulations of K.P.K. concerning social organisations in confrontation with judicial practice reveals doubts. The basic question that arises in this respect is, for example, whether the rules currently in force oblige the social representative to maintain objectivity, which should be characterised by the action of the public-interest spokesman. With regard to the social representative, the provisions of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 2013 laying down detailed rules for the application of Regulation (EC) No 45/2001 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Regulation (EC) No 45/2001 of There is no rule of law that would prevent a social organisation from participating in a process that could have its own interest in a specific case. There is also no provision that does not allow a person in a close family or personal relationship to participate in the process as a social representative with any party to the process or who may benefit personally from the outcome of the proceedings. Applicants shall not equip social organisations with any new powers that could allow them to achieve more fully in the process the objectives for which they have been set up. The proposed change, which excludes a judge from examining the validity of the organisation's accession to the process, may lead to an extension of the procedure - as the previous speakers have already pointed out - and also create new opportunities for deliberate obstruction of the process. The Supreme Court has stressed several times that the admission of a social representative to judicial proceedings should be treated as a rule in judicial practice, but not as an exception. Therefore, one of the two roads should be chosen in this respect. One is to equip the social organisation with real powers similar to the powers of the supplicator to help implement the constitutional principle of participation of the social factor in the process in a more fuller way, while the other is to adapt the provisions to the existing facts, namely simply to remove the provisions in question from the c.p.k. The proposed change does not bring any positive aspects in this regard. Concerning the amendment of the Article. 357 and Art. 360 c.p.k., there is no doubt that the transparency of the criminal trial can positively affect the judicial authorities by increasing the level of their work. Ensuring the transparency of the main hearing shall also promote the achievement of the objectives of criminal proceedings as set out in Article. 2 § 1 point 2 of the Commercial Code, namely through an appropriate criminal response and the disclosure of circumstances conducive to the disclosure of the offence, the appropriate impact on the strengthening of respect for the law and principles of social co-life and the prevention of other offences. However, the question arises as to whether the applicants' intention is to increase the transparency of the process, whether it is perhaps another stage of interference with the autonomy of the judiciary. According to the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party in the Code of Criminal Procedure, the principle of transparency of judicial proceedings, as expressed in the article, has been fully highlighted. 45 Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which also corresponds to internationally accepted standards. The main hearing, which is the most important stage of criminal proceedings, has been kept public, and the circumstances leading to its exclusion are indicated at constitutional level - Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 - or as indicated in the Code of Criminal Procedure. We are talking about morality, state security, public order, the protection of private life or other important private interests. In the doctrine, it is considered that all the conditions, apart from the last of these, do not give rise to problems of interpretation and, in the case of another important private interest, the court has been left to decide whether it is necessary to exclude the transparency of the hearing in the present case. And this decision-making slack the applicants want to deprive the judges of. Therefore, we do not see the need to change the legal situation in this regard. As regards the amendments to the Article. 357 k.p.k., in the view of the applicants, provision § 1 in the wording so far makes the granting by the court of permission to record images and sound from the course of the hearing conditional on the occurrence of inconsequential reasons which make the decision on this subject - in its indisputability - often arbitrary. These conditions are not as vague as the applicant claims, e.g. Whereas the concept of a legitimate social interest in accordance with case law must be brought into line with the situation, [[♪ The bell ♪]] when the public is interested in the matter. In conclusion, the analysis of this project raises many doubts, but the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party requests that the project be directed to further work. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u492	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In answer to the questions raised by the honourable Member, the aim of the project is to increase the transparency of criminal proceedings, with the aim of increasing the role of social control over the exercise of justice. Such a move should be considered as appropriate, in particular with regard to the Article. 45 Constitution, which introduces the principles of transparency of the hearing. In the current system of criminal proceedings, the principle of allowing a social organisation to participate actively in proceedings, as well as the rules on the abolition of the transparency of proceedings, are, in fact, based on the autonomous decision-making looseness of the formation of the court. High Izbo! Criminal proceedings, including in particular criminal proceedings, are a dynamic process which cannot be foreseen for the individual elements and effects of the criminal proceedings. Therefore, there is no legal possibility to provide in the provisions of the law. such solutions as would fully regulate all potential events and elements of the hearing. It is therefore appropriate to provide for such conditions as are binding on the court and, on the other hand, allow the formation of the ruling body to maintain those regulations, powers which are designed to ensure the regularity of the process. In addition, the regulations introduced are a significant step forward with regard to the current situation, where, in fact, there is no practical guidance on the matters covered by the amendment. Currently, these cases are essentially within the jurisdiction of the court, but there are no general clauses on the basis of which the formation of the court is to take a specific decision on formal proceedings. This amendment fills this gap. In answer to another question concerning the rules for allowing media representatives to report on the hearing, in my opinion this is the right step, which gives the formation of the judge a real legal basis for ensuring the organizational order in the courtroom. A possible allegation that the court may ask the unfavorable journalists should be considered wrong, as such a situation may also occur at present. The Court of First Instance has guidelines when and how it may restrict access by media representatives where large attendance would prevent or impede the proper organisation of the process. In addition, it is worth pointing out that the amendment introduces an instrument to control the provisions of the formation of the court. A party who is dissatisfied with the decision, even if it concerns the exclusion of disclosure, shall be entitled to appeal to another composition of that court, therefore it is an additional guarantee which serves to fulfil the disposal of the Article. 45 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. With regard to the contested law, as regards the rules for receipt of correspondence by a postal agent - and here I also answer the question of a lawyer or counsel, it is necessary to amend here, because the current situation is dysfunctional, ordering the personal receipt of unanswered correspondence by a lawyer or counsellor. Consequently, either these entities actually receive personal correspondence from the postal operator or such correspondence is issued to unauthorised persons. Often they are, of course, employees of the firm, but the law is still being violated here. With regard to the scope of the changes relating to the admission of loved ones to medical secrecy of the deceased, they allow for an increase in the possibility of law enforcement by the nearest person or knowledge of the lack of grounds for both civil and criminal proceedings. According to the current rules, in order for the person closest to you to obtain information that is a medical secret, in practice he must initiate criminal proceedings or obtain access to evidence - and this is not always an effective way, although it involves the authorities of the State to often pointless and unobtrusive activity. The amended rules allow the immediate ones, taking into account their alleged consent, not to object to at least one of the people closest to them, to know the facts and consequently the legal situation of the case. The acquisition of such knowledge will often result in pointless and pointless criminal proceedings and, subsequently, civil proceedings not being initiated. To sum up, in my opinion, the proposed changes are much more precise than they are currently fulfilling the directives of the Article. 45 constitutions. In view of the above, I ask the High House to refer the contested bill to the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u494	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Road Transport Act, print No 459, aims to fulfil the obligations imposed by Regulation No 1071/2009 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 October 2009 laying down common rules concerning the conditions for the pursuit of the occupation of road transport operator and repealing Council Directive 96/26/EC as regards the establishment and operation of the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs. The deadline for the adaptation of national law expired on 31 December 2012. Most Member States of the European Union already have national registers and exchange information within the European Union. Art. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Regulation No 1071/2009 provides that each Member State shall keep the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs who have obtained authorisation from the competent authority designated by that State to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. The processing of data contained in the register shall be subject to scrutiny by a public authority designated for that purpose. On the basis of the proposed rules, the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will consist of the following records: entrepreneurs who are authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator, serious infringements as referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) b of Regulation 1071/2009, and of persons who have been declared unable to manage the transport operations of the trader until such time as the good repute is restored in accordance with Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 of the Regulation referred to above. The register shall be kept in the electronic system by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport. Data will be transferred to the register by the authorised authorities: the Chief Inspector of Road Transport, the Elders and the Mayors of Cities on the rights of powiats, the Provincial Road Transport Inspectors, the Provincial Police Commandants, Customs Directors, the Chief Labour Inspector, the Road Managers and the Head of the Border Guard. In accordance with the proposed rules, data collected in the register of undertakings authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator shall be publicly and publicly available, in addition to data relating to the date and place of birth and address of residence of the transport manager and the natural person authorised to carry out the tasks of transport manager on behalf of the micro-entrepreneur. On the other hand, data collected in the records of serious infringements and records of persons who have been found unable to manage transport operations shall be made available only to authorised authorities and the Internal Security Agency. The project provides that the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs will be established on 1 April 2017. This deadline for the creation of a national register is due to the adoption of a three-month deadline for carrying out a public contract on the selection of the operator of the electronic system and a six-month deadline for the creation and implementation of the system. In addition to the changes resulting from the implementation of the European Parliament Regulation, the proposal provides for other changes. These changes are e.g. Amendment of Article 7a providing for the possibility to submit information from the National Criminal Register on non-punishment for an offence involving an infringement of national law as referred to in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) and the regulations of the European Union, I apologise, No 1071/2002, mentioned in Article. 7d paragraph. 2 offences corresponding to infringements of national law as defined in that Community Regulation, amendment of Article. 7a laying down rehabilitation measures, amendment of Article. 32 allowing the main road transport inspector to suspend the issue of driver certificates, adding Article. 15a-15d providing for a procedure for traders who have committed infringements. The draft shall also include an amendment to the Article. 11 designating the main road transport inspector as the authority empowered to issue extracts and authorisations for the occupation of road transport operator, a change of a Union nature which is beneficial to the environment of road transport operators. It is further specified in the Article. 39 names of professional examination confirming professional qualifications. Amendment to Article 47a determines the own tasks of local government units. The article has been clarified. 87. 1 concerning the control of printouts from tachographs and manual records on record sheets and printouts. These documents are included in the annexes to the existing Act as being subject to scrutiny. All provisions of the draft law, in addition to those relating to the creation of the register, will enter into force 14 days after its publication. The legislative work in the Ministry has already been in progress since 2010, in the previous term of government - until December 2004. The work on the project continued until December, followed by a two-fold review by the Council of Ministers, but this examination of the project was postponed in July and September 2015. Therefore, the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction recommends that work on the Law in the Sejm and the Senate be completed as soon as possible so that the Act can enter into force. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u502	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! The Road Transport Act is a constitution of the road transport industry. This is an important piece of legislation which sets out the rules for carrying out business activities involving the carriage of persons and goods. The law dates back to 2001, but has been amended several times. This amendment, which the government has tabled today, is of special importance because the proposals contained in the proposal concern the implementation of European standards, which have been expressed in five legislative acts. The first is Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 October 2009 laying down common rules concerning the conditions for the occupation of road transport operator and repealing Council Directive 96/26/EC. The next Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council also dates from 21 October 2009 and concerns common rules for access to the international road transport market. The next one also comes from that day, concerns common rules for access to the international market for coach and bus services and amends Regulation No 561/2006. The next two documents are documents already from the Commission: the Commission Decision of 17 December 2009 on minimum data requirements to be entered in the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs and the Commission Regulation of 16 December 2010 laying down common rules for the interconnection of national electronic registers of road transport undertakings. Why is this presentation important? Because it unifies in the countries of the European Community the rules on registers to eliminate from the market those entrepreneurs, those drivers, those carrying out transport activities who should not be involved in the carriage of persons or goods. These registers will be cross-border, these registers will allow for data exchange between countries. The important thing is that we proceed quickly with this bill, which is why on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club I request that the bill be submitted to the Infrastructure Committee for further work. Currently, proceedings on the loss of good repute are being initiated when the transport manager is convicted of crimes involving, for example, working time of drivers, driving licences, vehicle overloads, technical and other tests, as well as e.g. for offences arising from the regulation of commercial, insolvency, road traffic law, as well as those relating to wages and conditions of employment, professional responsibility or trafficking in human beings or drugs. However, it has so far been made clear that a good reputation may be lost in the event of intentional crime. However, this project deletes the statement that it is only intentional crime, and this is an error by the applicant. Instead of helping Polish transport companies, the Polish state adds further difficulties, thus becoming part of the economic struggle and in practice being on the side of foreign companies. EU legislation does not impose an obligation on us to limit these crimes only to unintentional ones. It is a provision voluntarily introduced by our government, only a question of what it is to serve. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u505	Thank you very much. Mr Mirosław Suchoń, the Modern Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u506	Thank you. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Modern Club on the draft law amending the Road Transport Act, print No. 459. The proposed proposal is largely due to the need to transpose the provisions of European Union legislation into the law of the Republic of Poland. My previous speakers have already mentioned these acts quite precisely, so I will not discuss them in detail, but I would like to point out that it is really urgent because of the need to implement these provisions - after a while - so that we will certainly take an active part in the work of the committee in order to implement these provisions into Polish law as soon as possible. In discussing our position, I would like to mention a few aspects, as this project, as implementing the necessary solutions, should, as I have already mentioned, be placed under the deliberations of the committee responsible, where we will look at it in detail. In line with the requirements set out in the relevant acts, the European States are required to implement certain mechanisms in the field of road transport, which, according to the European institutions, are ordering this area of the economy. They indicate in particular that Member States keep the National Electronic Register of Road Transport Entrepreneurs who have obtained authorisation from a State designated by the competent authority to pursue the occupation of road transport operator. In accordance with Union legislation, the relevant data contained in the national electronic register are also to be available to all competent authorities of the Member State concerned. The draft law on which we are proceeding contains provisions that will enable the proper register to be established and the obligations related to the sharing of data to be carried out. This register shall be kept in the electronic system by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport. The draft states that the register will consist of the following records: firstly, economic operators who are authorised to pursue the occupation of road transport operator, secondly, serious infringements, thirdly, persons who have been declared unable to manage the transport operations of the trader until the return to good repute. In accordance with European legislation, Poland, like other countries, should take all necessary measures to ensure that national electronic registers are interconnected and accessible throughout the Community. The draft law provides that the task of the contact point will be carried out in this respect by the Chief Inspector of Road Transport. Interestingly and rationally, at the same time, because it has not always been so, the bill contains a provision allowing the chief inspector to take immediate action to establish a national electronic register, including the execution of a public contract, i.e. to spend funds before the legislation creating that register enters into force. However, there is a question here concerning the Ministry of Infrastructure, as well as transport, and its information systems. Why didn't the government decide to set up a single system to carry out new tasks, including existing systems, i.e. central records of infringements and Transbit? These systems could be powered by Central Registration and Information on Business Activity and National Court Register. In the explanatory memorandum to the bill it is quite laconic, but it seems that this is not an explanation. For the record, this is no change from previous years. I ask this because Minister Streżyńska has repeatedly indicated in her speeches that only by combining these systems can we use the economies of scale and stop wasting public funds. Unfortunately, in this situation there will be another system for several tens of millions of zlotys, and in fact it will perform in part the same tasks that other systems perform. Now, back to the rules, I want to see a change going in the right direction. The amendment consists in the addition to the powers of the principal inspector of the right to issue a discharge from the authorisation granted to pursue the occupation of carrier. Currently, a first-time applicant for this authorisation is unable to obtain a discharge because the Chief Inspector does not have such competence. The amendment will, first of all, define these competences, second, the fees for issuing the discharge will be lower. In addition, the proposal also contains other adaptation provisions, which, as I mentioned earlier, will be discussed in detail and to which we will refer in further work in the committee. Which is particularly valuable in Amendment 4. The Senate has supplemented the law on provisions to correct personal data contrary to the content of the judgment which do not correspond to the provisions in the relevant documents or which do not comply with the facts. On the other hand, the 5th amendment extends the scope of the data contained in the registration card by the court. It is appropriate to agree with the reasoning put forward by the Senate that Amendment 6 has become necessary in view of the obligation laid down in the Act to publish the image of the person included in the register in order to identify the person in error. Amendments 7 and 8 concern inter-temporal cases and are, in our view, necessary for the correct implementation of the new rules. Already in the 17th century, they carried out nursing activities in hospitals that were established at monasteries. After World War I, one of the first nursing schools was founded in Poznań. In Poland, nurses and midwives are the largest professional group in health care and pursue the profession of public trust. According to the data of the Supreme Chamber of Nurses and Obstetrics, 221 thousand work in our country. Nurses and 26,000. midwives. Nursing and obstetrics are special professions, which are accompanied by each of us from the moment of birth until the end of our lives, requiring professionalism, which consists of excellent substantive preparation, systematic improvement of qualifications, but also, as rarely in which profession, having certain characteristics: unique sensitivity, empathy, caring, patience, unselfish kindness and respect for the dignity of another person, which, no matter what the circumstances, allow to act for people who are sick, disabled, vulnerable, needing care, but also support in unimaginable sometimes suffering. The focus and division of attention, organizational skills, in particular in the field of self-employment and the work of the team, and perception, particularly in the area of rapid and thorough recognition of the status of the subject, are also considered important psychophysical features relevant to these professions. An extremely important feature is moral responsibility, which requires feeling and tact towards the patient, and the obligation of professional secrecy. In addition, it should be stressed that nurses and midwives perform professional tasks in a diverse environment: hospitals, clinics, homes and outdoors, often in accidents or disasters. It is a work of a special nature due to: emotional burden, great responsibility for the patient's health and life, continuous availability, night work, the need to operate complex and expensive medical equipment, the problems arising from working conditions such as stress, the risk of infection with infectious diseases, physical exercise, congestion of the movement system and the risk of aggression from dissatisfied patients or their families. This makes it a very mentally incriminating competition. The financial situation of nurses and midwives is also worrying. We are seeing a rapidly growing shortage of nurses and midwives operating in the health care system. They emigrate for profit and work outside the country where they are valued for their knowledge, experience and expertise. Unfortunately, in Poland the role of nurses and midwives is marginalized and labour costs are underestimated. Hard working conditions and increased responsibility do not encourage them to take up or continue their professional practice in the country. Ladies and gentlemen! Nurses and Nurses! Midwives! ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u582	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I want to make a statement regarding the anniversary of the 750th anniversary of my beloved city, the beautiful Koszalin. 750 years ago, Koszalin was founded. I have the honour to represent today in the Sejm of the Republic of Poland 100 thousand Baltic cities with Slavic, Pomerania, Prussian, German and Polish past. The name Koszalin, like the name Pomerania, is Slavic, because the city was founded on Slavic land, which was already part of the state of the Piast rulers of Poland: I, Bolesław Chrobre and Bolesław Krzywoustego. The history of Poland, like the history of Koszalin, is inextricably connected with Christianity. Just as Poland begins its state existence from the baptism in 966 Koszalin begins its urban history with the Bishop's localization act in 1266. Nowy Koszalin was located on Lubecki law, which emphasized his relationship with the sea and maritime economy. He was part of the bishop's dukeship, a kind of theocratic republic, within which the city enjoyed full autonomy. I'm gonna have to cut it short because I'm a little short on time, and that's a shame. The city developed for centuries, seeking to benefit from the seaside position, developing the fleet and maritime trade, belonging to the Hanseatic League, acquiring further coastal estates. In 1446 it fought a victorious battle against Kolobrzeg, fighting the rights to maritime trade. There have also been less boastful episodes in the history of the city: discrimination against the Slavic population in cities and official restrictions on their rights. In Prussian and German times, the Jewish population was included in racist arrangements. In 1945. Pomerania has become the arena of the greatest battle fought by the Polish army since September 1939, a battle occupied by a huge tribute of blood. Breaking the Wał Pomorskie, fighting for Walcz and Mirosławiec, heavy buoys in the vicinity of Wierzchow Złocieniecki, Drawska, the battle for Kołobrzeg, fighting in the area of Dziwnow - these are just some episodes of this campaign. As part of the Pomeranian operation, Koszalin was captured, becoming a Polish city with time. With all the historical baggage, Koszalin received after the war the first visitors from Gniezno, Pomerania, Wielkopolska, as well as other areas of the country, repatriates from the East, from the West, Warsaw, who could not return to the destroyed capital. First of all, the reconstruction of the city, which was destroyed by the city's war activities, was started, and then its rapid expansion. In 1972, after the normalization of relations with the Federal Republic of Germany, a new Koszalin-Kobrzeska diocese with the bishop's capital in Koszalin was established. Today it is a modern, European, well organized city, still fulfilling traditional maritime ambitions, a city full of greenery and souvenirs of the past, but most of all beautiful neighborhoods and settlements. Koszalin - the city from which Poland should be proud, because it is in Poland that Koszalin has experienced the greatest, greatest period of its flourishing. Wonderful location between the sea from the north, the highest elevation in the southern Baltic - Chełmska Mountain from the east, dense coniferous forests from the south and beech forest from the west, with many lakes and rivers, midland manor houses and palaces makes Koszalin one of the most beautiful towns in Poland. However, it is still difficult to get here, because large road investments tend to avoid our region. In Koszalin there is another generation, for which the city is a homeland, a place on earth, ancestral land. A few years ago, Koszalin based its borders on the seaside lake Jamno, becoming a maritime city, investing millions in the Polish Coast, in water regulations and hydrotechnical buildings, benefiting itself and Poland's maritime potential. On the occasion of the 750th anniversary of the city, bearing in mind the historical experiences of this land, the good and bad moments of the past, I would like to express in the Sejm of the Republic of Poland the deep hope that not only Koszalin will be surrounded by a caring homeland, bringing its benefits and giving it a shine, but also Poland will see its chance in Koszalin, a unique city, open, hospitable, devoted and faithful Poland. Polish, be proud of Koszalin. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u592	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! People of Opole! People of the Bank! The beautiful historical city of Brzeg in Opole became one of the symbols of fighting the communist apparatus of the state, apparatus of repression, with the Church and the Catholic religion on the so-called. The Recovered Lands. The events which I want to raise in this House today took place on the morning of 26 May 1966 a few weeks after the 1000th anniversary of Poland's baptism. The state headed by Gomulka decided to use almost all the apparatus of repression available to him to prove to the public that the people's power does not intend to yield to the faithful, and especially against the power of faith and Catholic values which shaped the Polish statehood for 1000 years. It was then that the faithful in the Bank wanted to defend the rectory, the popular vicar, which the communists decided to pick up the local parish and allocate it for purposes, as it was then known, serving the general working class. The dramatic conflict about the vicar was the result of the decision of the communist authorities, which decided in 1959 to take over the objects belonging to the Church in the Western lands. In the case of the Bank it was a building near Castle Square 2, adjacent to the Museum of Silesian Piasts and the chapel of St. Jadwiga, where several priests lived at the time. On the premises there were clergymen who were trained in a seminary, who, according to the communist security apparatus, were a potentially threatening element of the state's system. The City Council Bureau in the Bank already tried to pick up the parish building in 1965. The official pretext was to put a plan in the medical facility. Priests were to use another building where the parish law firm functioned. Further appeals, curia interventions and talks with Members of the Sign, who were to convince the communists to withdraw from the decision, ended in a fiasco. In May 1966, priests received an eviction order. On 26 May, the communist authorities decided once and for all to resolve the Brest problem so that the news of possible riots would not cause tensions in the country, and so intensified, according to the authorities, the celebrations of the 1000th anniversary of Poland's baptism. In this regard, it was feared that the Catholic faith in society would be strengthened even more. When on the morning of May 26, the faithful gathered in Castle Square to protect priests from being expelled from the vicarage, the Esbians and the zombies began to pacify them. A few days earlier, ZOMO troops from Wrocław, Katowice and Opole, a total of 350 officers, were brought to the Bank. In the local prison, 100 places were prepared for potential convicts, and military barracks in the Bank were prepared to accept additional zombie meals. In the Castle Square there was a regular battle between the faithful going in procession and the ZOMO troops. Then the events moved to the nearby market, where police and SB made arrests. The communist apparatus of repression worked extremely quickly and efficiently. In a matter of hours, 80 people were arrested, seven of whom were sentenced to prison sentences. Several hundred people were injured. At the authority's command, they were refused to help them. In those memorable days of May 1966, the only time was when we had to write untruth about the victims and the kind of help we needed not to expose the victims to repression - one of the Brest doctors wrote in her memories. According to official records, more than 80 people were detained, several dozen participants were fined and seven were brought to justice. The highest sentence was: 2 years in prison for attempting to overthrow the Polish People's Republic and actively assaulting representatives of the state authorities. After the action of May 26, the authorities, in order to avoid embarrassment, implemented their plan. At the cost of PLN 2 million they rebuilt the rectory and opened a clinic there. High Seym! Here, in this Parliamentary Chamber, this statement on the 1050th anniversary of Poland's baptism and the 50th anniversary of the Brest events I would like to pay tribute [[♪ The bell ♪]] Heroes of anti-communist speeches, who stood up for the dear values of our nation's identity throughout the century.
