ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u594	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union of Individual Farmers (Solidarity) was registered on 12 May 1981. Jan Kułaj was the first president of the union. This function was performed until the time of internment after the declaration of martial law. It was the first such relationship in the history of Poland. NSZZRI.Solidarity was created by numerous initiatives of opposition groups operating in the countryside. The events in Radom and Ursus and the unfair agricultural policy for individual farmers had a significant impact in 1976. Working committees of peasant self-defense in many voivodships and numerous anti-communist opposition have joined the organization of the trade union of farmers. Pre-war PSL activists played a particular role, e.g. Mr Stanisław Mierzwa or Adam Bień. The first meeting of all independent peasant movements took place in September 1980, when the Founding Committee of Rural Solidarity was separated. The chairman of the committee was Zdzisław Ostat, the statute was drafted by Henryk Bąk. This was possible due to government commitments made during the August agreements on the establishment of sustainable prospects for the development of peasant family farms, equal treatment of particular agricultural sectors and equal access to means of production. In line with farmers' demands, provision was made for the authorities to create the conditions for the rebirth of rural self-government. Many other grassroots initiatives have been developed in the process of creating a trade union. The Dobrzyn region's peasant trade union was established, the People's Thought Centre, the Organization Committee of the Local Association of Agricultural Producers and other organisations at regional level. There has been a difficult time to counter divisions and try to unite. All attempts to register in the voivodship court of these unions met with a refusal. During this period, the authorities promoted the machinery rings as a sufficient rural government. Also, attempts to unite these three major groups have failed. The National Council of the Solidarity Agreement was set up, but the authorities tried to prevent the loss of control in the agricultural sector. In December 1980 the 1st National Congress of Agriculturalers of Rural Solidarity took place with the participation of 1500 delegates. The strikes in Rzeszów and Urzych Dolny affected the adjournment of the legal registration of the union. The postulate of registration of the union was supported by the Church and especially by the Primate of Poland Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński. Despite discussions between Cardinal Wyszyński and the authorities, the registration of the relationship was still refused. Numerous demonstrations of farmers took place in Warsaw and other localities in Poland. A delegation meeting of all agricultural unions was held in Bydgoszcz and a committee was set up to prepare a reunion. The renewed talks with the government party in Rzeszów and Ustrzyki Dolny ended with the signing of the agreement in February 1981. It is estimated that the farmers' union was around 900,000. Members. A great success was the registration of a united union on 12 May 1981 by the Voivodship Court in Warsaw. The registration was preceded by a mass in the Warsaw Cathedral with numerous participations of the rural community. The date of registration of the relationship coincided with the anniversaries of the death of Józef Piłsudski and General Władysław Anders, which escaped the attention of the communist authorities. 35 years have passed since that event. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u606	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! 1 April this year The Sejm accepted the so-called. a decounization law, i.e. a law prohibiting the promotion of communism or another totalitarian system by the names of buildings, objects and public utility devices. Following the adoption of the bill by the Senate, it was sent to the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda for the purpose of signing. According to the above legal act, the names of streets, bridges or squares given by units of local government cannot in any way commemorate or promote persons, events, organizations or dates which are symbols of communism or other totalitarian system. Names referring to persons, organizations, events or dates symbolizing repressive, authoritarian and non-sovereign system of power in Poland in 1944 and 1989 are also considered to promote communism. Self-governments will be obliged to change the names commemorating communism within 12 months of the date of entry into force of the law. The decision to annul the resolution giving the name of buildings, objects or public utility objects incompatible with the new provisions will be taken by the voivode after consulting the Institute of National Remembrance. More than 25 years after systemic changes in Poland still up to 1.5 thousand. The streets may bear the names associated with communism. In the country we have streets commemorating PKWN, the streets of the 40th anniversary of the Polish Republic, the Red Army, Władysław Gomłka, the Polish Workers' Party or Karol Świerczewski. It is an affronting heritage for Poland and Poles. The need to regulate these issues should not create any political controversy. Not without reasons in the article. 13 The Constitution states that the existence of political parties and other organisations referring in their programmes to totalitarian methods and practices of Nazism, fascism and communism is prohibited, and the Penal Code in Article. 256 considers the promotion of a fascist or other totalitarian state system to be a prohibited act. Any promotion of communist symbolism is demoralizing for society. We can't accept honoring people and institutions who don't deserve it. Sometimes it can be heard that sometimes the communist street patrons are less famous underground party activists or scientists who have allowed propaganda to exploit the results of their research and work. It also happens that the names of street patrons, well known to the older generation, commemorating the times of the Polish People's Republic, tell nothing to young people. Sometimes the patrons of the streets, schools, or buildings are simply unfamiliar or unknown. If the residents have no knowledge of the patron of their streets, they should be given it to them. This is a huge distinction. However, we definitely cannot promote any names that are associated with communism and merits for the Polish People's Republic and the Polish People's Republic. We should disseminate knowledge about people deserved for the latest history of Poland, not connected with any totalitarian system. People who could be honored with the patronage of the street, the square or the building, were not lacking in our country. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u617	Ladies and gentlemen! Today I wanted to briefly tell you about the Gombrowicz International Festival, who has been visiting my hometown for years. Since 1993 General Theatre im. Jan Kochanowski is the organizer of the Gombrowiczowski International Festival. The festival was initiated by Wojciech Kępczyński, director of the theatre. I will mention that this is one of the most important Polish theatre festivals. The event combines theatre, science, music and widely understood art. In the early editions, Rita Gombrowicz, widow of the deceased writer, took the patronage over her. The idea of the organizers was to create an international, multicultural forum for the exchange of thoughts, experiences, interpretations and rewording of Gombrowicz's problem, the problem of reading and reading his works. The event is very popular with the audience as well as critics. The jury composed of critics, directors and theatre theaterists evaluates numerous performances. The surroundings of Radom are the family sides of the writer, hence the choice of the city of Radom to replace the festival, which takes place regularly every 2 years. Witold Gombrowicz was born in the palace in Podradomska Vsola, where the Witold Gombrowicz Museum is located today. This is where the first literary ideas were born. As a result, the writer's work contains a number of references to towns near Radom. This year the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage is to transfer 150 thousand. PLN of co-financing for the 12th edition of the International Gombrowicz Festival.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u618	Declaration by the Parliament on territorial cohesion of the West Pomeranian Voivodship Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Recently, various concepts of creating on the basis of the existing Koszalin, Pilski and Polski voivodships, the new self-government Koszalin or Central Pomeranian voivodeship, have appeared in public space. This meets the opposition of the local community, expressed among others. in the resolutions of the Sejmik Voivodship Zachodniopomorskie, the councils of the municipalities and powiats of the Zachodniopomorskie Voivodeship calling for the rulers to maintain the territorial cohesion of the voivodship and thus the foundations of the self-government reform of 1998, the main assumption of which was the creation of strong and financially independent regions. Separation from existing voivodships of lands to a large extent structurally weak and the creation of Koszalin or Central Pomeranian voivodship based on them would not only lead to the establishment of a self-government unit which would be unable to conduct independent development policy, but would also weaken the potential of other voivodships. There were no new arguments for returning to the disqualified concept of additional voivodship in 1998. This initiative is anachronistic and harmful not only for the regions of northern Poland, but also for the whole country facing the challenge of rational and efficient use of funds from the budgetary perspective of the European Union for the 2014-2020 period. It is also of great importance that the creation of a new voivodship would entail a significant change in the system of implementation of European funds in Poland. All strategic and implementation decisions of the European Union's regional policy and the objectives and scope of the intervention of the European funds for the 2014-2020 period relate to the current administrative structure of the country. It would take at least a few years to adopt and negotiate the relevant programming documents, such as the revised partnership agreement, territorial contract and regional operational programme for the new voivodship. It should be recalled that the regional government is based on the community of its inhabitants. Since the creation of Western Pomerania, systematic actions have been undertaken to ensure spatial and economic and social cohesion of Western Pomerania. National regional policy should continue to be based consistently on regions with adequate development potential. Further strengthening the role of regions in developing development policies is part of the current debate on the future of European Union regional policy, the principle of subsidiarity promoted since the beginning of the European Union, the principle that every level of authority should only carry out those tasks which cannot be effectively carried out by the lower level or by the individuals themselves operating within society. This is also consistent with global trends that confirm that only strong regions based on strong intra-regional centres are able to effectively overcome development barriers and inspire growth. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-12-sejm-18-2.u619	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Year by year the population of beavers grows uncontrollably in Poland. These animals are getting bolder and bolder in the land and are destroying agricultural production. The dams created by beavers on rivers and watercourses cause water to be poured into crops and disrupt local drainage systems. This phenomenon is mostly affected by the region of the voivodships: Warmia-Mazurskie, Podlaskie, Lublin and Podkarpackie, but it occurs practically throughout the country. The owners of ponds, farms and gardens are helpless. Regional environmental directorates, hunting circles, agricultural chambers and local authorities are also powerless to deal with this problem. Resettlement of bobrzy families is an operation so expensive that it is performed only in isolated cases. A number of reservations are also raised by the system for estimating damages and the amount of compensation charged. Today in the Seym we started work on amending the Law on Hunting Law, and I hope it will be a step in the right direction that will settle issues related to compensation for damage caused by wild game. However, the problem of a rapidly growing population of certain animal species, especially beavers, remains to be addressed. The current Hunting Law and the Law on Nature Conservation and Animal Protection do not fulfil their function of limiting the extent of damage to agricultural crops. This problem was dealt with, among others. Lithuania, where beavers are protected only in national parks. High Izbo! If we do not take action to halt the continuous growth of the beaver population, this problem will grow very rapidly and result in further multi-million losses to farmers and the state budget. Thank you.
